Mr Marshal of the Sejm at its meeting on 8 February 2017, pursuant to Article. 87. 2 in conjunction with Article. 90. 1 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm, addressed the above draft law to the Extraordinary Commission for amendments to the codifications for its consideration. The Commission, after considering this draft law at its meeting on 23 February 2017, requests the High Sejm to adopt the attached draft law. The Commission introduced a supplemental amendment to the title of the Law on assistance to persons entitled to maintenance of 7 September 2007 in view of the introduction of an article in that draft. 2 giving a new wording to Article. Article 5 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3b point 1 of the Law on Assistance to Persons with Maintenance Allowance as follows: If the decision to consider a maintenance debtor as non-compliance with maintenance obligations becomes final, the competent authority of the debtor shall submit an application for prosecution for the offence referred to in Article. 209 § 1 of the Criminal Code. According to the principles of legislative technique, if only two laws are amended, they should both be mentioned in the title of amending law. The period of vacathio legis was preserved as it was in the original project, i.e. the law shall enter into force 14 days after the publication of the notice. Also all the content of the revision of the article. 209 § 1 of the Penal Code was adopted by the Extraordinary Commission, i.e. this basic form of non-alination offence as defined in Article. 209 § 1 of the Criminal Code, qualified form of § 2, also applications for prosecution of non-alimentation offences may be made by victims, by social assistance authorities or by bodies taking action against a maintenance debtor. And what was the most important of this amendment, § 5 and 6, which refer to the so-called. active grievance of the perpetrator, and deadlines for determining the payment of outstanding maintenance - 30 days in pre-trial proceedings since the first hearing and also 30 days in court proceedings where the court is required to waive the penalty, but there is also an exemption: unless the guilt of the perpetrator and the social harm of the act speak against the withdrawal from the penalty. So there's a new, special premise for a judge's sentence. I ask the High House to adopt the report and to pass this law. Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-22-sejm-38-1.u8	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Sejm Club of Law and Justice, I present the position of the Sejm Club on the revision of the Article. 209 Penal Code, which is non-alination crimes. A few general comments. The government project aims to improve the efficiency of the enforcement of maintenance benefits by eligible persons and to eliminate undesirable phenomena in the form of deliberate repeal of maintenance obligations. Here this purpose in the record of persistence has been eliminated as a mark of the crime. To date, the persistence and inability to satisfy basic life needs in the form of a basic non-alination offence have been eliminated in favour of a more objective criterion in the form of maintenance arrears equivalent to at least three periodic benefits or a delay in the provision other than a period of at least 3 months. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: Article 209 (2) of the Criminal Code introduces a type of qualified non-alination offence. Namely, more severe criminal liability - up to 2 years of imprisonment - will be subject to those who, failing to comply with the maintenance obligation of § Article 1 209 The Penal Code exposes a person entitled to the inability to satisfy basic needs. Here again, I stress that this is a change, it is a departure from previous regulation. This is a qualified figure, which is defined in § 2, i.e. the inability to satisfy basic needs. The amendment raises no greater doubt, even as regards the provisions of an inter-temporal nature - the offence of non-alimentation is a permanent offence, it consists in creating, maintaining a specific criminal effect, thus determining whether the provisions of the old or new law are applicable in the relevant state. 4 § 1 of the Criminal Code, i.e. a law more relative to the perpetrator of the crime should be applied. Added in Article 209 § 5 and 6 of the Penal Code aims to eliminate the criminal effect by paying in full the outstanding maintenance, both during the preparatory and judicial proceedings. In the first case, the perpetrator shall not be punishable if, before the expiry of 30 days from the date of the first interview as a suspect, he has paid in full outstanding maintenance, i.e. the proceedings are terminated and, in the second case, at the stage of court proceedings, the court waives the sentence, unless the perpetrators' faults and the social harm of the act speak against the waiver, the penalty shall apply. This amendment also contributes to the strengthening of subjective rights resulting from the Article. Article 71 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 the Constitution, which provides for specific State aid to families in financial and social difficulties. I also wanted to emphasise the need for this amendment with a certain statistics, which is also included in the justification for this government project. There are more than 50,000 cases per year in the courts. maintenance judgments, including about a third of the cases, are in the form of a settlement. 612,000. enforcement and maintenance cases were at the end of 2015. 13,000. persons are sentenced to imprisonment for the offence of non-alimentation - this is the data for 2014 - and only 3,700 people were employed on average from those convicted. The debt of maintenance debtors vis-à-vis the maintenance fund amounts to PLN 10.5 billion. The maintenance fund's expenditure amounts to 1.5 billion a year. High Izbo! All this justifies the need to amend this provision. [[♪ The bell ♪]] I move that this bill be passed. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

In the added § 1a Penalty increases to 2 years if a person obliged to pay alimony exposes a person entitled to an inability to meet the basic needs of his or her life. From the data I have, it's 1.5,000. the persons convicted of the offence for imprisonment are not in prison because of the absence of a place. The question is whether deprivation of liberty actually fulfils its role and is a threat to people who are notoriously not paying alimony. Those in detention for non-payment of the elements are also a double burden on the state budget, namely their arrears are paid by a maintenance fund financed by the State, i.e. taxpayers, and secondly, taxpayers bear the cost of keeping such a person in prison. As I said, the proposed solutions tend to tighten the penalties for abetting alimony. I have doubts as to whether the proposed changes will reduce the phenomenon we are talking about will make maintenance debtors more likely to pay alimony, and that they should pay, of which we do not have any doubts. I think that in the future we should also consider changes to the law, let's call it less repressive, which would make people obliged to pay alimony to do so. Perhaps a change in the criterion for determining the amount of alimony should be considered, setting it on the basis of the net income of a person burdened with alimony rather than an assessment of earning opportunities, as is currently the case. However, I agree with the view that the proposal under discussion today will not ensure full recovery of maintenance obligations. Yes, the idea of imprisonment will certainly bring some slow debtors to order. Under this kind of pressure, they will pay their debts. However, this amendment does not solve the question of people who intentionally prescribe their assets to other family members and hide their income only to avoid paying child support. Preceded regulations need to be introduced As soon as possible, however, they should only be the beginning of changes sealing the entire maintenance system. Action is also needed to change the attitude of the public towards those with maintenance arrears. I find the situations in which such criminals perform important social roles and enjoy this respect to be puriozal. About Free and Solidarni will support the project in its entirety. Thank you. ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-22-sejm-38-1.u36	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I present a report to the Extraordinary Commission on amendments to the codifications concerning the amendment of the Penal Code and certain other laws, i.e. print No 1231, the report is included in print No 1343. The Commission, after considering the draft law at its meetings on 9 February, was a permanent subcommittee meeting on amendments to the Criminal Code, and the Extraordinary Commission on 8 March 2017, requests that this draft be adopted. The Commission has made amendments. The amendment concerns Article. 42 § 1 of the Criminal Code, this is the addition of § 1a as follows: The Court of First Instance shall prohibit the driving of any motor vehicle in the event of conviction for the offence referred to in Article. 178 or Article. 180a and in the case of Article. 244 if the act of the perpetrator was not to comply with the driving ban. The purpose of this amendment was to harmonise the rules on mandatory criminal sanctions in the form of a ban on all motor vehicles. In the current proposal, the obligation to give a ruling on this prohibition concerned the perpetrators of the offences of failing to comply with the decision of the competent authority to withdraw the right to drive, the criminalisation of that act was in Article. 180a of the Criminal Code, and so-called. escape from the road control authority, Article. 178b Criminal Code. However, it should be noted that by imposing an obligation to impose such a prohibition on perpetrators who violated the so-called. administrative ban on driving, consideration must be given to the criminal situation of perpetrators who have not complied with the prohibition on driving by a court. Such behaviour shall be validated by Article. 244 The Penal Code and the perpetrators who violated this criminal ban are not required to rule on driving bans from an axiological point of view. The prohibition on driving by courts refers to serious breaches of safety and traffic order, which are generally criminal. It is therefore not justified in the established hierarchy of values to rule on a mandatory criminal measure in the event of the perpetrator's failure to comply with the administrative decision to withdraw powers. In turn the omission of this decision in the event of non-compliance with a criminal measure of the same content as that decided by the court... And that's why we have an exemption from the article in this amendment. 244 No driving bans. I would like to remind you that in this article there are many violations of prohibitions and orders. Another amendment concerns the provision which has been introduced, namely the Article. 3th Act, which states that in the Code of Criminal Procedure in Article. 213 po § 1a the following paragraph shall be added: 1b as follows: In the land traffic crime proceedings referred to in Chapter XXI of the Criminal Code, information should also be obtained from the central driver records and from the records of drivers violating police traffic regulations concerning the defendant. If a period of 6 months has elapsed since this information has been drawn up, it shall be re-established. The introduction of a transitional provision shall be laid down in Article. 7, after the adoption of the report of the Permanent Sub-Committee and the Extraordinary Commission meeting of 8 March this year: on the matters referred to in Article. 3 This Act, initiated and not completed by the date of its entry into force, shall apply the provisions of the Act as amended by Article. 3, as amended by this Act. It is an obligation for the court to check, because sometimes these proceedings take longer than 6 months, it is a matter of asking the court to ask the CEPiK for information as to whether there was a suspected person or already accused at the stage of the judicial proceedings in the field of road traffic law. The Act will enter into force 14 days after the date of publication, with the exception of Article. Point 5, which will enter into force on 1 January 2018, and let me remind you that this article deals with changes to the forms of criminal mandates and the allocation of the series and numbers of criminal mandates generated by the ICT system. Here, of course, there must be time to prepare these forms. The Minister for Foreign Affairs will be responsible for this. public finances. Maybe I will give a brief indication of what solutions in this project raised doubts or differing legal views. First of all, it is a change in the case of a localised or factual situation located in the Article. 188b validating the behaviour of persons who, despite being issued by means of audible and light signals by a person authorized to control traffic, a moving vehicle or on a ship or an air command to stop a mechanical vehicle, do not stop the vehicle immediately and continue driving. I would like to stress here that such behaviour has so far been a misdemeanor. Currently, due to the large number of such situations - in public space there was information regarding non-compliance with the audible signals of those persons who serve to control traffic, escapes often ended with accidents, destruction of property, destruction of roadside stops, fences, it sometimes ended with severe damage to the body of other road users, and unfortunately also fatal cases - the penalization of such behaviour seems to be most justified. There is a question of this kind - which should be pointed out - that this crime can only be committed on purpose. It's not like you're perverting an involuntary crime, i.e. due to recklessness or negligence, that someone, for example. for technical reasons, he could not stop the vehicle immediately and continued driving. I would also like to draw attention to the conjunction that these two signals - sound and light - must occur together. If only one is used, the conditions shall not be met, the marks in Article. 178b. There was also doubt about the change in the article. 2 concerning the limitation period under the Code of Offences. To date, the term has been measured in such a way that the criminality of the offence has ceased if a year has been counted since it was committed. This could have been extended to 2 years, but sometimes the period of initiation began just before the expiry of the limitation period and those who were guilty used it in different ways, extending the procedure, and often already at the stage of the appeal proceedings, those offences were statute-barred and the courts of appeal at times, when the proceedings at first instance lasted longer, had to issue decisions dying. This amendment is now changing, the course is counted on the initiation of proceedings and then the criminality ceases 2 years after the end of that period. This is done so that in these cases it is possible to give substantive decisions, not to waive proceedings as a result of limitation. Thank you very much for your attention and I ask the High House to adopt the report.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-22-sejm-38-1.u44	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Poselski Club I am proud to present my position on the report of the Extraordinary Commission on amendments to the codifications of the Government draft law amending the Penal Code Act and some other laws, print No 1343. As indicated by the justification of the project, the main objective of the proposal is to increase road safety by increasing the effectiveness of the prosecution of certain offences and traffic offences. The project assumes among others.: recognition as a crime of escape from a traffic-control chase carried out by a person, tightening of criminal sanctions in the event of an accident resulting in death or serious injury to the body, the perpetrator of which is intoxicated or under the influence of an intoxicating agent or fugitive from the scene, tightening of criminal sanctions in the event of driving after the court has issued a ban on the driving of those vehicles or extending the limitation period for criminal offences. The doubts raised during the work of both the subcommittee and the committees concerning the Article should be divided. 178b Criminal Code introducing a new type of criminal offence consisting only of the failure to comply with the recommendation of a person authorized to control traffic. Thus, the criminality of such an act is to take place regardless of the mode of driving, the conditions of the road or the occurrence of a safety risk in road traffic. There is no doubt that the changes made to the Penal Code are primarily populist. These are changes for which the sole purpose is to increase the criminality of road traffic by tightening criminal repression. Projectors assume that the more severe the punishment, the more effective it is. This is incompatible with the principle of rational punishment. You want to solve security problems on Polish roads only by intimidating citizens, potential perpetrators, with the dimension of penalties. However, there is no action by the government to prevent and introduce solutions to improve road safety. At the same time, you introduce a mandatory judgment by the courts of a criminal measure in the form of a ban on driving any motor vehicle in the cases referred to in Article. 178b or Article. 180a. This significantly interferes with the scope of the judicial authority, which may lead to a reduction in the possibility of a free assessment of the circumstances of the case and the extent of the penalty appropriate to the degree of social harm to the act. The Modern Posel Club therefore hopes that, in addition to criminal measures, the government will also take preventive measures to increase road safety. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-22-sejm-38-1.u62	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! On behalf of the Freedom and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position towards the government's draft law amending the Penal Code Act and some other laws. Unfortunately, in Poland we still take traffic regulations too lightly. Without fear of penalties, we break various kinds of regulations both as drivers and as pedestrians traffic participants. However, disregarding the basic principles in this regard can have serious consequences, including death. An important issue that arises in the context of the Traffic Criminal Code is the sobriety of drivers. According to data in our country, there are people who sit behind the wheel of vehicles after drinking alcohol and under the influence of various types of drugs. A similar danger on the road is posed by pedestrians who are intoxicated. The existing rules do not provide sufficient protection for all traffic participants against violations of the law. In my opinion, the reasons should be sought in the existing possibilities of delaying proceedings and as a consequence avoiding liability for offences, as well as in too mild penalties which do not deter either before the first or before the next traffic offence. Therefore, I consider it a very effective measure to improve road safety to strengthen penalties for road offences and traffic offences. The project also regulates, as I stressed in my earlier position, the limitation period for criminal offences. In its amendment, the government has shortened the limitation period for traffic offences and assumes speeding up the ruling in this case. The positive consequence of these changes will be the inevitability of penalties for violations of road rules. I hope that this will contribute to improving road safety. The Act is also intended to prevent too frequent the phenomenon of driving a mechanical vehicle after withdrawal of rights. No one needs to be convinced that these are necessary solutions for proper enforcement. The amendment also regulates the controversial question of measuring the body's alcohol content or the presence of a substance similar to alcohol. The clarification and tightening of traffic legislation is, in my view, the right direction for the government to act. The Free and Solidarnia Members' Circle fully supports the proposed project and calls for changes to the procedated law as soon as possible.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-22-sejm-38-1.u64	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! I have the honour to present on behalf of the Committee on Justice and Human Rights a report on the Government's draft law amending the Law on the Employment of Freedomless Persons, print Nos 931 and 1351. The purpose of the presentation, as the Council of Ministers points out, is to increase employment among persons imprisoned in penitentiary units. It is intended to contribute to the implementation of an important project, which is a work programme for prisoners. The most important element of this bill is the increase in the lump sum that would be attributed to an entrepreneur employing convicted persons. The Sejm at its meeting on 4 November 2016 pursuant to Article. 39 (1) (a) 2 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm referred the draft law to the Committee on Justice and Human Rights for consideration. At the committee meetings on 29 November and 8 March, this project was examined. The relatively long gap between meetings was due to the expectation of a positive decision by the European Commission, which had to give a positive opinion on State aid, since the increase in the lump sum which is available to the companies employing convicted persons is treated as public aid in the European Union. Written opinions for the project were presented by the State Labour Inspection, the Supreme Court, as well as the Polish Craft Association. No comments were received in these opinions. The meetings of the committee were attended by the Minister, the State Secretary at the Ministry of Justice, Patrick Jak, together with his colleagues, as well as the staff of the Justice Committee's office and the Legislative Office. In addition, Colonel Artur Dziadosz, Deputy General Director of the Prison Service, together with his colleagues, and Henryk Pawlaczyk, President of the National Council of Curators, also with his colleagues took part in the meeting on 29 November. In addition, Mr Marcin Wolny, representative of the Helsinki Foundation for Human Rights, was also present at this meeting. Michał Zoń, Head of the Legal Office of the Central Board of Prison Services, together with his colleagues, and Waldemar Salinski, Chairman of the Committee on Ethics and Disciplinary Affairs of the National Council of Curators, together with his colleagues, was present at the meeting on 8 March 2017. At both meetings of the committee, the project was presented by Minister Patryk Jak. There have been discussions about the project. The draft was systematically discussed at its meeting of 8 March. As part of the amendments tabled, the amount of the lump sum allocated to entrepreneurs employing prisoners sentenced from 40% to 35% of the value of the prisoners' wages was reduced, which is intended to increase the funds of the Convicted Professional Activation Fund and the Development of Incarceration Works. Vacatio legis was also shortened to the first day of the month following the month of the announcement, which is intended to prevent problems in calculating the lump sum for entrepreneurs. In addition, amendments of a regulatory and legislative nature have been tabled. 13 Members voted in favour, no one opposed, no one abstained. After considering the draft law, the Committee on Justice and Human Rights requests the High Sejm to adopt the draft law as proposed in the committee's report, print No 1351. I would like to thank all ladies and gentlemen, legislators and experts for their constructive cooperation. In the event of specific questions, I shall remain at the disposal of the High House. Thank you. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-22-sejm-38-1.u65	Thank you very much, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. And first, Mr Bartłomiej Wróblewski, Club Law and Justice. You're welcome, sir.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-22-sejm-38-1.u66	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! I have the honour to present on behalf of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice my position on the report of the Committee on Justice and Human Rights on the Government bill amending the Act on the Employment of Freedomless Persons. Since the beginning of the examination of this law, we have agreed with the aim of the Council of Ministers to prepare this project. This objective is to increase the employment of inmates in penitentiary units. Thus, the project contributes to the implementation, in our opinion, of an extremely important work programme for prisoners. It is true that work should be an important part of the execution of imprisonment, because it has a positive effect on the sentenced, contributes to their rehabilitation, allows for the development of their professional skills, and is also an opportunity for them to earn money. The work programme for prisoners has an additional advantage, namely it has a positive effect on the order of prisons, on order in penitentiary units. The draft law amending the Law on the Employment of Freedomless Persons leads to changes in the way in which the funds collected in the Professional Act are spent by increasing from 20% to 35% of the lump sum available to businesses for the increased cost of hiring people without freedom. This will bring employers' current costs closer to those of previous legislation, in particular those that were still in force in 2010. The increased labour costs are currently not sufficiently compensated, which is the main reason why the lower level of employment of convicted persons has persisted for several years. The reduction of these costs increases the attractiveness of such work for entrepreneurs, which should translate into an increase in the employment of people without freedom. We believe that this is a worthy objective. In our assessment, the changes made during the examination of the project in the Justice Committee improve the bill. In particular, we consider it reasonable to shorten the vacathio legis from 6 months to the first day of the month following the month of the announcement, which is intended to prevent problems in calculating the lump sum available to businesses. We also agree with other legislative amendments and changes of a legislative nature. Therefore, on behalf of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, I declare that the club supports the adoption of the bill by the Sejm.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-22-sejm-38-1.u74	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! In accordance with the current wording of the implementing Penal Code, no remuneration shall be payable to the sentenced person for the purpose of ordering and auxiliary work for the organisation of the Prison Service or for the purpose of ordering the local government within a maximum of 90 hours. After the planned amendment, this provision is to be supplemented, inter alia. about work for social purposes for entities for which the authority of the municipality, district or voivodship is the body of the founding, state or local organisational units and companies governed by commercial law with the exclusive participation of the State Treasury, the municipality, the district or the voivodship. Social work will also be possible for institutions or organisations representing the local community and educational institutions, youth educational centres, youth sociotherapy centres, medicinal entities within the meaning of the provisions on medical activities, organizational units of social assistance, foundations, associations and other institutions or public utility organisations providing charitable assistance. This means expanding the number of entities for which prisoners could work free of charge. As she recalled, now only 40% Inmates shall carry out works of a paid nature. At this point, we should ask whether we should also look for solutions to increase the ability of prisoners to take paid work instead of merely expanding the number of persons entitled to work free of charge. An attempt to respond to this challenge is the subject of the project under discussion today to increase the financial capacity of the Convicted Professional Activation Fund and the Development of Incarceration Works by increasing from 25% to 45% deductions for this purpose from the remuneration of the convicted person. The lump sum is to be paid at the request of entrepreneurs. This solution should encourage businesses to employ the convicted more than they have done so far. The additional funds of the fund are also intended to be used for the construction of production infrastructure in the penitentiary units. Doubts about reducing prison pay should dispel international statistics. Poland as one of the few countries currently offers 100% of residents minimum wage for work, while in France and the UK it is 44%, while in Sweden and Hungary it is about 1/3 of the minimum wage. Greater than previously, the deduction for the Professional Activation Fund of convicted prisoners will leave the prison pay to a higher level with regard to the minimum wage than in those countries. Unfortunately, often inmates who want to work, so they express their desire to rehabilitate through work, do not have that possibility. The employment rate of prisoners in Poland is one of the lower in Europe. In Western European countries it reaches around 60%. In our country, according to the Ministry of Justice, this rate has increased from 35% to 42% since the beginning of 2016, with 68% of the sentenced in 2010. Currently, 27 600 prisoners are employed. ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-22-sejm-38-1.u96	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present on behalf of the Committee on Education, Science and Youth and the Committee on Economics and Development a report on the governmental draft law on amending the Law on Measurements and the Law on voivodeship and government administration in the voivodship, which was included in print No 1195. As indicated by the applicant, the project concerns 24 amendments. As a result of the changes, the legal nature of the validation institution would be clarified and the legal process of metrology control at the place of installation or use of measuring instruments would be improved. However, the most important changes proposed by the government are: introducing the possibility of applying the statistical method in the legalization of a homogeneous batch of measuring devices, setting up a Metrology Council which would be involved in the election of the President of the Main Office of Measurements, reforming the field structures of the Main Office of Measurements and identifying additional tasks of the President of the Main Office of Measurements. A few words about the process of the project. The project entered the Sejm on 4 January 2017. Subsequently, on 11 January 2017, it was sent to the first reading held at the 34th meeting of the Sejm on 25 January 2017. The Sejm sent the draft to the joint committees: the Committee on Education, Science and Youth and the Committee on Economics and Development. The joint committees, in turn, directed the project to the subcommittee. At the subcommittee meeting, the draft was assessed and the subcommittee proposed two substantive amendments, one of which concerned the possibility of commissioning the Metrology Council to draw up opinions and analyses. Two further substantive amendments were proposed at the meeting of the Joint Committee on 8 March 2017. The first of these amendments concerns the requirement that people on the Metrology Council must have a university education. In turn, the second of these amendments concerns the definition of the conditions to be met by the university, from which a scientist may be appointed to the Metrology Council. In addition to government representatives, the chairman of the Main Office of Measures took part in the work of the committee and the subcommittee. The result of the work is a draft law included in the report of the Joint Committees in print No 1360. The work in the joint committees was constructive, efficient and substantive, in a good atmosphere. The joint committees: the Committee on Education, Science and Youth and the Committee on Economic Affairs and Development, after considering the draft law, request that the High Sejm adopt the draft law as proposed in print no. 1360. I would like to thank the ladies and gentlemen for their cooperation, as well as all the experts and participants in the committee's work. Thank you. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-22-sejm-38-1.u97	Thank you very much. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Paweł Lisiecki will perform on behalf of the Law and Justice Club. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-22-sejm-38-1.u98	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present on behalf of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice a position regarding the report of the Committee on Education, Science and Youth and the Committee on Economics and Development on the governmental bill amending the Law on Measurements and the Law on voivodeship and government administration in the voivodship, which is included in print No 1195. The project presented concerns changes in one of the most important but not known areas that affect everyday life. The law of measurement concerns metrology, without which it is difficult to talk about any statehood. The main task of metrology, i.e. learning how to measure and how to interpret the results obtained, is to ensure the fairness and certainty of economic turnover, and with the development of industry also technical and production security. The legislative amendments proposed by the government aim to carry out a number of different tasks. First of all, they aim to clarify the legal nature of the verification institution, to improve the carrying out of legal metrology checks at the place of installation or use of the measuring instrument, to eliminate interpretation doubts, to allow the re-certification of measuring instruments legally placed on the market and used not only as a result of conformity assessment, and finally to introduce the possibility of re-certification of a homogeneous batch of measuring instruments so-called. the statistical method and its definition. In addition, the project envisages simplifying the field structure of the measurement administration by transforming existing regional offices into local districts. The number of out-of-town departments is not expected to change in relation to the number of existing out-of-town offices. The current three-level structure with narrow local character makes it difficult to use existing resources of measurement administration flexibly and efficiently and complicates decision-making processes. This is because at the moment there are as many as 58 circuits, each of which should work formally only in the area of its properties. In addition, the draft law provides for the establishment of a long-standing Metrology Council, which would bring together experts working with the Main Office of Measurements. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice supports the draft in print 1360 presented in the committee's report and will vote in favour of it. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-22-sejm-38-1.u123	We will proceed to the third reading of the bill in the voting block. We proceed with the examination of item 5 of the agenda: Report from the Committee on Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation on the Government's draft Working Time Act on IWT vessels (Prints No 1321 and 1342). I ask Mr Andrzej Kobylarz to present a report to the committee. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-22-sejm-38-1.u124	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! I have the honour and pleasure to present a report by the Committee on Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation on the Government's draft Working Time Act on IWT vessels. The draft law is intended to implement the law of the European Union. This Act implements Council Directive 2014/112 of the European Union of 19 December 2014 implementing the European Agreement on certain aspects of the organisation of working time in inland waterway transport concluded by the European Inland Navigation Association, the European Organisation of Captains and the European Transport Workers' Federation. The draft law concerns persons working on inland waterway vessels employed under a contract of employment as crew members or cabin crew. The proposed changes result from the need to harmonise and improve working time on inland waterway vessels and improve working conditions for persons working during navigation. They will also allow for regulations for inland fleet managers similar to those for shipowners in the Member States of the European Union. The EU Directive obliges Member States to adopt provisions governing certain aspects of working time in inland transport, working conditions on ships and relating to the health and safety of workers. The implementation of the provisions of the Directive will allow for a uniform system of treatment of workers working on inland waterway vessels while introducing a new concept of working time on inland waterway vessels into the Polish legal order. The concept of working time on inland waterway vessels in the extended definition of the Labour Code will be understood in the sense of carrying out business duties not only on board the vessel, because in inland waterway transport the working time of the worker on board is not equivalent to working time on inland waterway vessels. This includes administrative and legal formalities, the current passenger service and the employee's readiness to do work during the forced stop of the unit. Thus, readiness to work will become a period of time included in the working time on an inland waterway vessel. In addition, the new rules indicate top-down working hours limits with overtime on inland waterway vessels. The maximum number of working hours with overtime shall not exceed 14 hours in each 24-hour period and 84 hours in each 7-day period. The maximum working time during the season shall not exceed 12 hours in each 24-hour period and 72 hours in the 7-day period. The Directive also regulates the maximum working time on ships during the night, which covers 7 hours between hours. 23 a.m. 6 a.m., which makes a significant difference in terms of the provisions of the Labour Code. The maximum working time during the night shall be 7 hours in a 24-hour period and 42 hours in a 7-day period. In view of the above, after first reading and considering this draft law at the meeting of the Committee on Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation on 9 March on behalf of the committee, following its positive recommendation, I propose that the attached draft law be adopted. Thank you. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-22-sejm-38-1.u125	Thank you very much, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. I would ask Mrs Dorota Arcisewska-Mielewczyk to speak on behalf of the Law and Justice Club. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-22-sejm-38-1.u126	Thank you. Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! On behalf of the Law and Justice Club, I will present my position on the Government's draft Working Time Act on IWT vessels, print No 1321 and 1342. The draft IWT Working Time Act aims to implement Council Directive 2014/112 implementing the European Agreement on certain aspects of the organisation of working time in inland waterway transport, concluded by the European Inland Navigation Association, the European Captains Organisation and the European Transport Workers' Federation. This directive obliges Member States, as the previous speaker said, to adopt rules governing certain aspects of working time in inland waterway transport, health and safety on inland waterway vessels. The Directive should be implemented in the Polish legal order by 31 December 2016, but this deadline has been extended. This is due to the need, due to the nature of the regulation, to mandatory wide public consultations and arrangements. Such comments were received, among others. from the Association of Polish Inland Shipowners. Also, due to the socio-financial impact of the Ministry of Maritime Affairs and Inland Shipping, it was necessary to consult the Ministry of Family, Labour and Social Policy and the Minister of Finance and to examine them in depth and in depth. At the same time, due to the deadline laid down in the Directive, the Ministry of Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation remains in constant contact with the European Commission, preparing an explanation on the above. the deadline. The current provisions of the Labour Code do not fully meet the needs of shipowners and workers in the inland waterway sector and are in practice difficult to apply due to the specific nature of the work carried out on inland waterway vessels. The implementation of the requirements of the Directive into Polish law will ensure that Polish shipowners have the same conditions for organising work on inland waterway vessels as those of other European countries. This will also respond to the needs of shipowners and workers in the inland waterway transport sector and introduce regulations that take into account the specific characteristics of work on inland waterway vessels. The design rules will apply to persons working on these ships as crew members or cabin crew but will not include persons who independently and on their own account operate vessels - they will only apply to persons working on inland waterway vessels employed under a contract of employment as crew members or cabin crew. The most important regulations introduced by the draft law include the inclusion of readiness to work until working time, the introduction of a 12-month reference period for employers and the introduction of working time limits for workers working on inland waterway vessels, namely the limits on overall working time on inland waterway vessels, working time limits on passenger ships as well as working time limits at night. The above limits shall not apply in situations where inland waterway vessels and persons employed on inland waterways are at risk, or where assistance is necessary, in emergency situations, to other vessels and persons present on those vessels. The project also concerns the provision of an adequate number of rest days for workers employed on inland waterway vessels and the obligation for the employer to keep records of working time on inland waterway vessels. The draft law only introduces an additional obligation to keep the records on board the vessel for 12 months. In view of the obligation to implement Directive 2014/112/EU, the current monitoring by the European Commission of the state of implementation of the Directives by the Member States, as well as, as regards the proposed law, the nature of the regulations in place that meet the needs of the environment, workers and employers of the inland waterway transport sector, the Law and Justice Club will support the adoption of the above mentioned legislation. the project. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-22-sejm-38-1.u127	Thank you very much. On behalf of the Civic Platform Club, Mr Arkadiusz Marchewka will perform. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-22-sejm-38-1.u128	Thank you kindly. Dear Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Civic Platform Club, I will present my position on the report of the Committee on Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation on the Government's draft Working Time Act on IWT vessels. First of all, I would like to say that in Poland general provisions are applied to workers working on inland waterway vessels, which are primarily based on the Labour Code Act. At the same time, the Labour Code does not meet the needs of operators in the shipping industry and in practice is difficult to apply due to the specificities of the work carried out on inland waterway vessels. The draft law therefore provides for the implementation of the Directive of 19 December 2014, which implements the European Agreement on certain aspects of the organisation of working time in inland waterway transport, concluded by the European Inland Navigation Association, the European Organisation of Captains and the European Transport Workers' Federation. The implementation of this Directive aims at harmonising and improving working time on inland waterway vessels and improving working conditions during shipping while aiming at such regulation for inland waterway fleet managers as shipowners have in other Member States of the European Union. The implementation of the Directive is also intended to ensure a uniform system of treatment for workers working on inland waterway vessels throughout the Community. The new solutions introduced by the draft law compared to the Labour Code relate primarily to working time in inland waterway transport. According to the project, the working time also includes the period of readiness to work, e.g. the ship's on-call time and the waiting time for loading or unloading, the working time shall therefore be counted against the working time. In addition, total working hours with overtime in inland navigation, together with preferentially granted hours of rest in relation to the Labour Code, will be introduced. The limits laid down in the draft also include working time during the season on a passenger ship and working time at night. When describing this draft law, it should be noted that these regulations will not directly affect the working conditions and remuneration of seafarers, in particular the operating system of the ship will not change, the number of hours considered to be overtime or carried out at night will not change, nor will the number or number of mandatory documents requiring regular registration change. As regards the impact of this draft law, it should be noted that the project concerns two groups of entities. The first group are inland waterway shippers, i.e. about 500 companies operating in the field of inland waterway transport, and these are estimates presented on the basis of the data of the Central Statistical Office, as well as on the basis of a document prepared specifically for the procedure of this law containing an analysis of the social, financial and legal consequences of the provisions of this directive in Poland. The second group affected by this draft law are persons working on inland waterway vessels. It is a group of about 600 people and it should be noted that in Poland in inland waterway transport there are mainly small shipping companies, often family-owned, in which workers mostly benefit from the so-called. self-employment. It should also be noted that the implementation of this directive in Polish legislation will not have direct financial consequences for Polish shipowners, which is very important, and that there is no need to change the current contracts and the costs of adopting the proposed solutions by the entrepreneur. Making sailors' working time more flexible is a way of responding to the needs of shipping operators in this respect. I would also like to point out that I consider it very important that, during the process of this bill, experts from recognised and reputable research entities such as the Maritime Academy Innovation Centre were used - this is a very reliable entity, and I am pleased, Minister, that this expertise has been prepared in this process. [[♪ The bell ♪]] At the same time, given the need to implement EU law into Polish law, the Civic Platform Club will support this bill. Thank you.

The first clause of the Annex to the Directive, concerning the scope of application, notes that the draft law has disappeared from the extremely important wording concerning the directive, which limits its scope to justified and understandable cases. Art. 1 of the draft law reads: "The law shall apply to persons engaged in work on an inland waterway vessel employed under a contract of employment (...), whereas the directive states: "This agreement shall apply to mobile workers (...). The absence of this key wording on the route makes the provisions of the Act, including Article. 11 on working time records would even apply to intra-port shipping when crews operate even several ships a day during a change, including so-called. small, where there is often no possibility to store such documents at all. Secondly, a comparison of the provisions contained in the so-called directive. Clause 12 with the corresponding Article. The 11th draft law, to which observations were submitted at the committee's meeting, makes it possible to see the validity of the observations submitted, fully in line with the spirit and letter of the provisions of the Directive, and the non-compliance with those provisions of the draft, paragraph 4 of the article. 11. The provisions of the Directive require that the working time of crew members be recorded in the form of registers in such a way as to enable the employer to monitor compliance with its requirements by requiring that they be kept on board at least until the end of the so-called ship. the base period, the records of which are to be subject at appropriate intervals, by the end of the following month at the latest, to joint scrutiny and approval by the employer or his alternate and by the employee. These records are clear in the light of the practice of sailing on routes in Western Europe, where changes in crews occur in several weeks' cycles, e.g. 2 in 2 or 3 in 3 weeks, and after the change, staff travels all over Europe to their homes. Therefore, joint checking of records is to be carried out, I quote, at appropriate intervals, and therefore appropriate for the work cycle of the employer concerned, but, again, I quote, by the end of the following month at the latest. The Commission's argument is not confirmed by the provisions of the Directive, as nowhere in the Directive requires the employer to provide the worker with a record of working time by the 10th day of each month, as such a provision contradicts the established practice of replacement of crews on routes. So that, High Izbo, your marshal, on behalf of the Kukiz Posel Club15, we would like to present three amendments to this law and we believe that they will really remove this overinterpretation of the directive and this will certainly contribute to the normal functioning of inland waterway transport. Thank you kindly.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-22-sejm-38-1.u132	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Draft Act on Working Time on IWT Ships. The purpose of the adoption of the new rules is to implement the requirements of Council Directive 2014/112/EU of 19 December 2014 implementing the European Agreement on certain aspects of the organisation of working time in inland waterway transport, concluded by the European Inland Navigation Association, the European Organisation of Captains and the European Transport Workers' Federation. It should be noted that in accordance with the Article. Article 4 (4) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. 1 Member States were required to implement the laws, regulations and administrative provisions necessary to comply with that Directive by 31 December 2016 at the latest, as mentioned at the outset of the Directive. According to the draft law, the working time on an inland waterway vessel will include the work carried out on board the ship at the employer's request. Staying ready to work on an inland waterway vessel will be counted against working time. According to the draft law, the working time on an inland waterway vessel must not exceed 8 hours per day and on average 40 hours on average in an average 5-day working week in a 12-month trading period and the weekly working time, including overtime, must not exceed 48 hours on average during the trading period. The maximum working time on an inland waterway vessel, including overtime, shall not exceed 14 hours in each 24-hour period and 84 hours in each 7-day period. However, the maximum working time during the season on a passenger ship, including overtime, shall not exceed 12 hours in each 24-hour period and 72 hours in each 7-day period. The project also introduces a maximum working time at night on an inland waterway vessel which shall not exceed 7 hours in any 24-hour period and 42 hours in any 7-day period. It was assumed that in inland navigation, the nighttime was 7 hours between hours. 23 a.m. 6 a.m. At the same time, adequate protection is proposed for workers who are considered unfit to work at night on the basis of a health certificate - to be transferred to work in accordance with the position taken outside the night. The proposed law introduces an obligation for the employer to keep separate records of working time on an inland waterway vessel for each worker. The employer is to keep such records until the end of the accounting period. In addition, by the 10th day of each month the employer shall provide the employee with records for the previous month. The staff member shall sign records the copy of which shall be kept for a period of 1 year. The Act is due to enter into force 14 days after the date of publication in the Official Journal of the Laws and Moderna will vote in favour of this Act, but I would like to draw the attention of the Minister for Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation and the Prime Minister to the fact that the Act should have been adopted a few months ago by the end of 2016. - and that such delays may be and are a real problem for the entities concerned. Thank you very much. [[Applause]] [[Chairman of the session includes Deputy Marshal Ryszard Terlecki of the Sejm]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-22-sejm-38-1.u144	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I would like to thank you very much for today's debate in the plenary room on the report on the government draft law on working time on inland waterway vessels. I would like to thank the rapporteur first of all and also the Members who spoke on behalf of the clubs and asked questions. I would also like to thank the Committee on Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation, at which the first reading of this law took place. As you pointed out, the draft IWT Working Time Act aims to implement Council Directive 2014/112/EU implementing the European Agreement on certain aspects of the organisation of working time in inland waterway transport, concluded by the European Inland Navigation Association, the European Captains' Organisation and the European Transport Workers' Federation. Implementation to Polish law by the Act on Working Time on IWT Ships ensures the creation of Polish shipowners of such conditions for organising work on IWT vessels as the ship owners of other European countries have. The draft law complements the current provisions of the Labour Code in a way more suited to the needs of shipowners and workers employed in the inland waterway transport sector and introduces regulations that take account of the specificities of work on inland waterway vessels. To answer your questions and the amendments tabled regarding the delay in implementation, I would first of all like to say that we are still in contact with the European Commission. The situation simply requires extensive consultation due to the fact that the association of Polish shipowners wanted to introduce an additional delegation of employees. This required consultation with the Ministry of the Family and the Ministry of Finance. It could be said that this went beyond this directive. There is therefore a slight delay. This will be by the end of this year, given that these consultations have been ongoing and require additional arrangements. As regards Mr Wolf and Mr Khrushch, the questions concern the amendments tabled. In response to the amendments, it should be noted that the Act was consulted, inter alia. with the largest Polish association of shipowners, which has developed an agreement with its European partners, which has in fact been introduced by the directive. The project raised no objections. We also have an opinion from the Ministry of Foreign Affairs on the compatibility of the project with European Union law, also with the directive being implemented. At the same time, it should be noted that the amendments tabled are unilateral and may discriminate against at least one group of inland waterway workers. For this reason, they should not be adopted at this stage. Amendment 1 concerns the addition of the en route expression and Amendment 2 concerns the definition of the en route expression. First of all, it requires that the main objective of the Directive, in line with the preamble, is to ensure the protection of the health and safety of workers in the inland waterway transport sector. The objective of the protection of workers therefore applies both to persons employed as crew members and to persons employed as cabin crew. The working time of the worker on board, as set out in the Annex to the Inland Water Transport Directive, is not equivalent to the life of the ship. The average working time on the ship does not only apply to the duration of the craft's voyage, but also concerns the activities of the ship's staff, e.g. carrying out minor repairs on board the ship, selling tickets, handling passengers or handling formal and administrative matters for the ship. Therefore, the introduction of the above amendments, i.e. the introduction of an expression: on the road and the definition of expression, would result in the exclusion from the protection of the scope of the Directive of parts of workers employed in the inland waterway transport sector, which would result in discrimination against some workers employed on passenger ships, for example.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-22-sejm-38-1.u145	Minister, it's on the committee that everything will be discussed, maybe details... Secretary of State at the Ministry of Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation Jerzy Materna: Yeah, but I just wanted to explain, because there were questions asked, among others. by Mr Wolf and... I know, I know. Secretary of State at the Ministry of Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation Jerzy Materna: ...and Mr Khrushch. That's why I'm explaining this. It also requires that the exemption is incompatible not only with the directive's objective of ensuring protection for all workers in the inland waterway transport sector, but also with the legal order in force. The provisions of the Act should apply to all employees, and not only to those who do work during the voyage of a craft lasting more than 2 days and in the A1 work system. The contested bill therefore meets the objectives set by the Directive as it applies to a broad category of workers in the inland waterway transport sector. In addition, according to the opinion issued by the Minister responsible for Polish membership in the European Union, the project was considered compatible with European Union law and the Directive implemented. Amendment 3 requires emphasis that the working time records are an employee document and are kept separately for each worker. Working time records shall be kept for the purpose of payment of remuneration and other work-related benefits. The obligation to introduce is defined by the Labour Code. Therefore, employers in the inland waterway transport sector keep records of working time for their employees, in accordance with the provisions of the Labour Code. The draft law introduces only additional obligations relating to the keeping of this register. One of these obligations is to provide the employee with a record of working time for the previous month. The control mechanism set out in the Directive also needs to be underlined. It is intended to control the amount of the payment of the salary and the work carried out by the employee. The 10-day deadline laid down in the draft law will allow the employer to prepare the data for the records without affecting the pay date and providing the worker with timely information on the amount of his remuneration. Depending on the employer's work cycle would be contrary to the objective of the control mechanism and would result in the worker being informed about his work and the remuneration due at irregular intervals. Such a change would lead to a deterioration in the situation of workers and discrimination against the labour market sector. It must be stressed that the provisions of this law have been consulted with the largest association of employers operating in inland waterway transport, the representative of which confirmed in the Parliamentary Committee on Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation that the provisions introduced are beneficial for both employers and workers. On the other hand, the changes proposed by Kukiz15 would lead to a deterioration in the situation of employees. ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-22-sejm-38-1.u146	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! On behalf of the Free and Solidarnia Circle, I am pleased to present my position on the report of the committee on the government draft law on working time on inland waterway vessels. The aim of the proposed law is to implement in Polish law one of the EU directives concerning the organisation of working time in inland transport. In accordance with its provisions, Member States were required by 31 December 2016 to adopt the laws, regulations and administrative provisions necessary to comply with it. This law was widely consulted and therefore Poland exceeded the deadline for implementation. The Act will undoubtedly improve the working conditions of persons working during shipping, and regulations on ship work will eventually be harmonised throughout the European Union. So far, the work on the ship has been regulated by various Polish laws, among others. relating to the daily and weekly standard of work, but also e.g. night work and Sundays and holidays. Following the implementation of the Directive, the more favourable working conditions in national law will remain and will be complemented by provisions that protect workers under the EU directive. This favorable solution is worth supporting. It is worth noting, however, that the provisions of the Directive apply only to persons working on inland waterway vessels under contracts of employment. The government draft law implementing the EU directive redefines work on inland waterway vessels and introduces a very important concept of a season which is of diametric importance in the context of passenger ships. A revised working dimension during the season will allow Polish shipowners to avoid interruptions in passenger service on their vessels managed by them. I do not need to convince anyone that this solution is very beneficial for shipowners and as such it is worthy of support. In addition to defining the number of hours and rest during the season, the limits of night work and Sundays and holidays as well as periods of rest are set. Clear regulation of working hours is beneficial for both managers and managers, as it allows us to predict what both parties can count on. Controversy during the work on the Act in the Committee on Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation has led to the need to keep records of working time on the inland waterway vessel. I agree with many voices that sounded during the committee's work that we have to take care of the employee's interests, and recording the time of his work is a general responsibility of the employer not only on board the ship. The contested law protects the interests of the worker carrying out the tasks entrusted to him on inland waterway vessels and should not raise any doubts. Wide public consultation, the need to implement the EU regulation on the Polish ground and the benefits for Polish workers are, in my opinion, sufficient arguments for the Free and Solidarni Circle to support further legislative work on the government law.

[[That's what I'm talking about.]] The proposed solutions have many advantages, for example some. The establishment of a network of hospitals begins the period of integration of various forms of medical services financed by public funds. Until now, applicants for the financing of the benefits covered by the competitions and consequently the settlements, hospitals had no incentive to focus on the real health needs of patients and, above all, to satisfy them comprehensively. In the new system, the main stream of resources will flow to hospitals profiled for many types of hospital care for health needs. The choice of these hospitals, i.e. their inclusion in the network, will be based on a number of criteria, which will provide patients with comprehensive hospital medical care. [[What about the Polish county?]] [[Very good with the Polish county. Very good.]] This will be adjusted every 4 years. The proposed change will end the age of unhealthy, sometimes fraternal competition, which has occasionally led to pathology, and the contracting entities, i.e. the voivodship departments of the National Health Fund, have potentially allowed protective decisions to be taken. There have been cases. The new solutions will make the financing of a very large and appropriately selected group of hospitals relatively stable, in principle eliminating destructive competition. Stable long-term financing of hospitals is crucial for patient health safety. [[It's like a speech.]] With the introduction of the new system, there will be no temptation for hospitals to be aware of benefits that are particularly favourable. This has always been at the expense of the provision of other hospital services, which put hospitals in difficulty holding many patient treatment profiles, so public hospitals were in a difficult situation, i.e. district, voivodship and clinical. The new system favours these hospitals, multi-profile hospitals, and also promotes the integration of hospital services with outpatients, night and holidays. There will be no financing uncertainty for primary hospitals. Units constituting the so-called The hospital network will be funded on a flat-rate basis, i.e. they will have their own budgets, and they will be able to manage them freely, thereby increasing the chance of providing patient-specific benefits. [[Mr Beat Małecka-Libera: Sure.]] The size of the first lump sum for a given hospital will depend directly on the price of the so-called. units of account, medical benefits and, of course, the number of benefits provided. [[So it's gonna count?]] The lump sum in subsequent years will depend on the lump sums from the preceding periods and, of course, on the number of benefits made. The amount of the lump sum will be set aside to finance healthcare services which, due to the need to ensure adequate access to them, require the establishment of separate funding. The list of these benefits will be determined by a regulation of the Minister of Health. In order to ensure that there is no doubt, at the stage of the Parliament's work to date, it was clarified that the first lump sum would be set at the date of entry into force of the amendment, so that this would be determined on April 2017 depending on the end of the legislative process. The draft implementing act to amend the provisions proposes an algorithm for setting a lump sum for a given period depending on the factor of increasing or decreasing the number of benefits granted during the preceding periods, but an additional legislative directive was introduced in the government proposal making the lump sum subject to the activity of hospitals. Perhaps this will result in an adjustment of the algorithm attached to the draft amendment. Since I am talking about the additions introduced by Members to the government project, it must be pointed out that there is also an exceptional provision for the annexation of contracts for the implementation of medical rescue tasks by mid-2018. Without this correction, there would be an obligation to conduct competitions in this field in Poland. just before the introduction of the so-called. hospital networks. This would mean that they would have to be annulled once the network has been put in place, which would, of course, be pointless. And a few more sentences about typing primary hospital security units. On the basis of statutory criteria, directors of the voivodship departments of the National Health Fund will draw up lists of hospitals so-called. the network, which will follow, as the rapporteur has already mentioned, by the end of June this year. or, in the case of merging entities, by the end of September. In addition, branch directors, after obtaining a favourable opinion from the Minister of Health, will be able to identify the hospitals concerned to the network despite failing to meet all selection criteria if they are relevant for the proper security of access to healthcare services. So the vast majority of today's hospitals will be in the so-called. hospital networks. First of all, this applies to public entities. However, the form of the competition of offers and negotiations will not be eliminated, will remain. Therefore, hospitals not qualified for the network will be able to apply for financing of their medical services. This will address the scope of identified health needs in the area concerned and will be based on the NFZ's plan to purchase benefits. We are at the threshold of the first fundamental change in the organisation and financing of the health system. The system has exhausted its capabilities so far. After many years of operation, he led, among others. to unacceptable dispersal of the provision of services and to unreasonable competition between health care units and to uncertainty about financing, in particular public hospitals. In fact, the negative effects of maintaining this system would have been much greater if it had not stopped contracting between hospitals and the NFZ voivodeship departments. However, it is impossible to annex contracts indefinitely. The introduction of a new system is therefore necessary. There must be a reformed system for providing medical services to rationalise health care in Poland and make it more effective. The Minister of Health proposes this in the interests of patients. The new system, on the one hand, promotes comprehensive patient care and, on the other, stabilises the operating conditions of hospitals, including county, voivodship and clinical hospitals. The proposed change in hospital treatment is not only the only but also, above all, a reasonable proposal. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-22-sejm-38-1.u167	Thank you very much, sir. I invite Mrs Anna Maria Siakrowska, Circle of the Posel Republicans.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-22-sejm-38-1.u168	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Republican Circle, I have the honour to present my position on the Government's draft amendment to the Law on Public-funded healthcare provision. The amendment proposed by the government assumes the establishment of a network of hospitals. Within this framework, health care providers will be classified in one of the six categories depending on the scope of their services. The introduction of the hospital network is also linked to changes in the rules for financing healthcare services. To date, the current principle of competitive conclusion of contracts in this area will largely be replaced by the principle of contracting entities eligible for hospital networks. What will be the result? The result is that more than 90% of the funds will go to hospitals in the network, and only 10% will go to hospitals. means... It will be possible to apply for these measures on a competitive basis. In practice, hospitals not qualified to the basic system of hospital health care insurance will have very limited access to the money from the state budget. Consequence? Many of these facilities may be closed down, and they will have to be closed down. The draft law submitted by the government also raises a number of concerns in health care environments. The commentators pay attention, among others. to a catalogue of medical profiles classified in the different categories of hospital networks. Outside the network there will be among others. Medical profiles such as geriatrics, psychiatry or rehabilitation. Just look at the content of the article. 95l, who doesn't mention these profiles at all. This could lead to a situation in which hospitals specializing in particular profiles will not be online. Consequently, this will lead to their bankruptcy. An example of such a facility, which may not be included in this network for these reasons, is the Silesian Centre for Reumatology, Rehabilitation and Prevention of Disability in Ustroń. Another question: qualifying county hospitals for the first category of the network. According to the assumption in this category, the hospital can only have five branches - this is precisely what Article refers to. 95l. Point 4 (1) of the draft law. Here it is worth noting that there are a number of county hospitals, e.g. district hospital in Grójc, which has more branches not foreseen for the first level of the network. If the bill enters into force in its current form, these branches, e.g. orthopedic or ophthalmologic, will not be included in the network. Another point is justified in our view by the fear that smaller hospitals or establishments that have medical profiles not included in the first degree of the network will artificially connect with other facilities, often located in other cities and considerable distances from each other. In this way, they will try to guarantee themselves the level of funding so far, appropriate to such facilities. Such regulation may therefore result in the creation of artificial and fragmented structures in which individual branches will be in a significant distance from each other, which will, of course, adversely affect the situation of the patient, even in another city. Some ministerial hospitals also appear to be at risk. MSWiA Hospitals in Jelenia Góra or Koszalin will not go online, although there are a number of responsibilities, e.g. in case of war. The reason is prosaic: they simply specialize in areas not included in the law, e.g. a hospital in Jelenia Góra dealing with psychiatry or a hospital in Koszalin, which does not operate on duty but performs scheduled operations. In conclusion, the government's proposal for an amendment, the draft law amending the law on public-funded healthcare provision raises so many doubts that members of the Republican circle will vote against the draft law as proposed. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-22-sejm-38-1.u170	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Free and Solidarni Circle, I will present my position on the report of the committee on the government draft law amending the Law on the provision of public-funded health care. The law under review is the reorganization of the whole health care system. In accordance with it the hospital care system will change, but as we all hope, the quality and speed of treatment of Polish patients will improve. The existing system for financing hospital services does not work properly and I don't think anyone has any doubts about this. The selection procedures for individual hospitals do not work and current time contracts with individual institutions are repeatedly annexed. The problem at present is the system of annexation of contracts, which makes hospitals unable to plan long-term human resources policies or larger investments. The reason is simple: they do not know whether they will still have funding and at what level. I'm sure the laws will change that. In the current system, no one is in principle coordinating comprehensive treatment of the patient and the entire recovery cycle. The introduction of new solutions will make patient care much more coordinated. The doctor will thus be more focused on the patient's health needs and not on the cost-effectiveness of the individual components of his treatment. This is supported by the basic system of hospital care provision introduced by the law, which separates six levels of the benefit insurance scheme to which hospitals will be classified once every 4 years. It is worth noting that the Act also makes it possible to annex contracts with the National Health Fund to those entities that do not enter the hospital network. The agreement for the hospital by individual decision has the right to initiate a branch director of the National Health Fund, who, after receiving a positive opinion from the Minister of Health, will be able to integrate this hospital into the network in his region. In the Act, the Ministry planned flat-rate financing of benefits that would allow for more rational spending depending on the needs of the area concerned. Of course, you can't overlook the shortcomings of the bill. I see three basics. According to the Sejm Analysis Office, the effects of the law cannot be predicted. The Ministry prepares this bill for a long time, but will it avoid the negative consequences of its impact? Secondly, I am concerned about the fate of hospital workers who will not enter the network and will not receive a contract with the National Health Fund through a competition.
