ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-22-sejm-38-1.u250	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on Health, it has been my honour to present to the High House a report on the government draft law amending the law on the collection, storage and transplantation of cells, tissues and organs, printing No 1332. The bill entered the Sejm on 28 February 2017. On 6 March 2017, the Marshal of the Sejm sent the above draft law to the Health Commission for first reading. March 21st this year. a first reading of the draft was held at the Health Committee at which the project was considered. The committee's report is in print No 1390. The contested bill concerns the amendment of the Act of 1 July 2005 on the collection, storage and transplantation of cells, tissues and organs. It should be stressed that the project is very technical. The main reason for the proposed changes is the need to implement the provisions of the EU rules into Polish law, thus establishing a uniform framework guaranteeing high quality and safety standards for cells, tissues and organs intended for transplantation and human use. The primary objective of the draft law is to adopt provisions to ensure that, irrespective of their intended use, cells, tissues and organs comply with certain quality requirements and equivalent safety standards. The draft law introduces standards for the introduction into the territory of the Republic of Poland from countries outside the European Economic Area of human tissues and cells intended for transplantation and human use, and introduces rules for the collection and testing of tissues and cells that take place outside the European Union, and measures to ensure monitoring of tissues and cells on the way to and from the donor. The draft law contains, among others, the following: provisions introducing a unique identifier used for tissues and cells distributed in the European Union in the form of a single European code which contains information on the main characteristics and characteristics of tissues. The adoption of the proposed measures in the draft law is subject to the implementation of Commission Directive (EU) 2015/565 and Commission Directive (EU) 2015/566, which provide that the provisions adopted on the basis of them should apply from 29 April 2017. The draft law indicates this day as the date of entry into force of the legislation. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! During the work of the Health Commission on the draft law at its meeting on 21 March this year. The four substantive amendments and the legislative and editorial amendments tabled by the legislator were adopted unanimously. The whole project was accepted without a vote of objection. The Health Committee therefore requests that this proposal be adopted. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-22-sejm-38-1.u251	Thank you very much, Member. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. I ask Mrs Józef Hrynkiewicz to speak on behalf of the Law and Justice Club. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-22-sejm-38-1.u252	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! On behalf of the Law and Justice Club, I have the honour to present the position of the club vis-à-vis the draft law amending the Act on the collection, storage and transplantation of cells, tissues and organs and draft implementing acts for printing 1332. The aim of the bill is to implement European Union law. According to the opinion put forward by the Government, the project is compatible with European Union law. The project shall contain a comprehensive, comprehensive justification and draft implementing acts. The Health Committee, at which the project was examined, also received two opinions from the Sejm Analysis Office. The main objective of the current project is to implement the provisions of the two 2015 European Union Directives, i.e. Directive 565/2015 and Directive 566/2015. The draft also amends the existing law to clarify the current provisions. According to the assumptions, the draft amends the Act of 1 July 2005. Its aim is to align Polish legislation with the provisions of the Directives mentioned above amending the 2006 Directive 86 of the European Communities with regard to technical requirements for the coding of human tissues and cells and to the provisions of Commission Directive 566/2015 implementing Directive 2004/23 of the European Communities as regards procedures for the verification of equivalent quality and safety standards for imported tissues and cells. The Directive was to be transposed into national law by 29 October 2016 and should apply from 29 April 2017. The contested law is to enter into force on 29 April 2017. An important part of this law concerns the new definitions of the basic terms in accordance with their wording in the directives implemented. The second group of changes in definition is based on a critical approach to the translation of concepts, medical terms used in European Union legal acts in order to bring them to a clear interpretation and to eliminate the ambiguity of interpretation of the concepts used so far. The Act also adds new concepts which are justified in the practical application of the provisions of the Act. The explanatory memorandum explains in detail the changes made, refers to the changes applied to the verification procedures and standards of quality and safety of the tissues and cells transported, and introduces a uniform European code for tissues and cells distributed in the Union and the obligation to ensure traceability of tissues and cells using a uniform European code from human collection to human use or removal. The Act also sets a 30-year period as a minimum to preserve data by tissue establishments. On 21 March, the Health Commission examined the draft law presented by the Government, tabled substantive, legislative and linguistic amendments and unanimously accepted the draft. The Law and Justice Club does not comment on the proposal, will vote in favour of it. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

Legal security and clearly defined rules protect patients and, in case of unfortunate complications, help victims to obtain help, not just medical help. For this reason alone, the proposed solutions are worthy of support. ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-22-sejm-38-1.u310	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I would like to present the position of Kukiz15 on the report of the Committee on Digitisation, Innovation and Modern Technologies on the Government's draft law amending certain laws in relation to the tasks of public administrations in relation to certain public registers, print No 1305. The Sejm at its 37th meeting on 8 March 2017 pursuant to Article 8 of the Rules of Procedure. 39 (1) (a) 2 Rules of Procedure of the Sejm referred the above draft law to the Committee on Digitalisation, Innovation and Modern Technologies for consideration. The Commission for Digitalisation, Innovation and Modern Technologies considered this draft law at its meeting on 8 March 2017. The Commission has not tabled any amendments. The proposed law provides for changes that will enable citizens to deal with a number of issues that require public administration without having to leave home through ICT systems. The proposed changes concern inter alia. rules on road traffic (the possibility of checking the number of criminal points via the Internet) and rules on the implementation of various family and assistance benefits (to make available information from the various systems which exempt citizens from adding additional documents to their applications, e.g. University or university education certificates). They also concern the revision of the Central Registration of Vehicles and Drivers regulations (e.g. The scope of the data to be collected in the central register of drivers shall include full data on the infringement - including the vehicle concerned: type, brand, model, registration number). The project provides for free access to vehicle data collected in the central vehicle register to environmental inspection bodies. In addition, the project includes a number of changes resulting from the 1997 analysis of the Law on Road Traffic in the 2015 version, which are necessary for the smooth start - still under preparation - of the CEPiK 2.0 system. . The proposed amendments are intended to increase access rights to the above mentioned. data from bodies such as the Environmental Protection Inspection and bodies determining the right to family benefits or assistance, which, according to the justification of the draft law, is intended to reduce the bureaucratic burden for citizens. The introduction of the facilitations proposed in the Act is now necessary and long-awaited in many respects. The rules provide a practical opportunity to facilitate citizens, whose result will be a reduction in the time needed to do some of the activities that the State forces them to do - so they carry out one of the many obligations that it has towards the citizen, namely to act in his favour (here saves time and facilitates access to data). For these reasons, the Kukiz15 will vote in favour of the committee's report.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-22-sejm-38-1.u311	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present on behalf of the club of the Polish People's Party our position on the report of the committee on the government draft law amending certain laws in connection with the tasks of public administrations in the field of certain public registers, prints no. 1305 and 1359. This proposal provides for the possibility for citizens to handle matters with public administration through ICT systems. Regulation provides for an amendment of the rules governing the operation of the central vehicle register, the central driver register and the legal framework for the launch of the CEPiK 2.0 system. . The project creates a legal framework for the launch of e-services for citizens allowing the Internet to check the number of criminal points received by the driver for traffic violations committed. Provisions in this respect - Article. The first point of the project - is to enter into force on 1 April 2017. Information on the number of points will be made available after the data subject has been certified with a profile trusted with ePUAP - based on data provided by the Police from the records of drivers in violation of traffic regulations. On the other hand, environmental inspection authorities will have free access to vehicle data collected in the central vehicle register. In the case of exports of waste in the form of a vehicle abroad, the responsibility for illegal shipment of waste shall be borne by the seller of the vehicle under the jurisdiction of the Republic of Poland. The above obliges the Chief Environmental Officer to determine the last owner of the vehicle being moved by waste and to carry out administrative action to that person to enable the decision to be taken in accordance with Article. 25 and Article 25 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 February 2011 laying down the rules and general principles concerning mechanisms for control by Member States of the Commission's exercise of implementing powers pursuant to Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council and repealing Council Regulation (EC, Euratom) No 1605/2002 (OJ L 298, 26.10.2012, p. 1). 26 Law on the International Movement of Waste. To date, the activities of the Environmental Protection Inspection have been hindered by the lack of access to data from the CEPiK system. The essence of the proposed amendments is also the reduction of the number of certificates which must be attached to the application by applicants for family benefits, benefits in the event of unsuccessful execution of alimony, the Large Family Charter and educational benefits. These changes will mean reducing the bureaucratic burden for citizens. In line with the proposed amendments, it will be possible to obtain independent access to the educational information system to bodies granting family benefits, benefits from the maintenance fund, the Large Family Charter and the education provision. The authorities granting family benefits, maintenance fund benefits and education will have access to the Central Insurance List. They will also be able to make direct use of the data collected in the insured account and in the premium payer's account, on the basis of social security rules. The project assumes that the authorities granting the Large Family Card will have access to the PESEL register and to the EXMOoN system. Designed changes are a continuation and development of existing solutions. They provide that the authorities carrying out family benefits, benefits in the event of unsuccessful execution of maintenance and the provision of education shall have independent access to income information from applicants for benefits or recipients and members of their families from the tax authorities or the minister responsible for public finances. The Act is to enter into force on 1 July 2017, with the exception of among others. records of the online verification of the number of points received by the manager and the collection from the educational information system by the Minister of data necessary to determine the right to benefits. High Izbo! The construction of e-states and digital Poland should be one of the priorities of our activity in the Sejm. This is what the 21st century requires of us and a rapidly changing environment in which we operate. And you have to admit that every year this is more and more a digital environment. Reducing bureaucratic burdens for citizens, facilitating their contact with offices via the Internet and improving the exchange of data between offices will facilitate the day-to-day functioning of citizens and improve the functioning of administrations. The comments made by the General Data Protection Officer to the project must not be disregarded. They concerned the security of the transmission and transfer of personal data. How do project authors intend to secure data so that they are not subjected to hacker attacks? It is also strange to write about access to databases for voivodships. Why is it that the voivodships are granted such broad powers? Speaking on behalf of the Polish People's Party, I cannot ignore the problem of unequal access to such projects in our country. We still have many online deserts - areas where even the best-formated e-government will not reach. In parallel to building a digital administration, we should expand fast Internet access in rural areas. In particular, the construction of fibre networks seems urgent. So we should go two-track: improve both the quality of the e-state and its availability, universality. Especially in rural areas.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-22-sejm-38-1.u325	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the government, I have the honour to present a bill amending the Repetition Act and some other laws. The amendment of the Repetition Act was primarily dictated by the very bad state of conduct of this repatriation. Since the 1990s, on the basis of the previous provisions of the Act, repatriation has been conducted only by local governments and in a rather imperfect way, because only local authorities had the opportunity to receive financial assistance if they invited repatriate families without names, while if the local government wanted to invite specific repatriate families using names, then of course it had to cover all costs itself. The repatriates actually had little financial assistance, just for the development and the train ticket, which nowadays is a bit of anachronism. Hence the very increased criticism of the Supreme Audit Chamber as regards the assessment of the effects of repatriation so far. Besides, many countries, many European countries, I think that here the Germans should be mentioned as an example, the repatriation was very effective already in the 1990s, and the beginning of the 2000s was actually the end of the repatriation process. As for the Poles who were deported, they were looking forward to repatriation, this process was very poor. About 6,000 were repatriated. people for more than 20 years. This process needs to be significantly accelerated, as there are people who have been waiting more than a dozen years to be repatriated. It is necessary that this process is actually accelerated. Accelerating this process we want to achieve by placing responsibility for the process of repatriation on the government, not counting only on the good will of the local government, and even if this good will was, only minor financial assistance was allocated to local governments. In line with our commitments to the elections, which are included in the electoral programme, but also confirmed by Prime Minister Beata Szydło's exposé, there is an amendment to the Repetition Act. The fact is that the work on this amendment has continued quite a long time, but we wanted to introduce a revision of the Repetition Act very carefully consulted. She was consulted with people who had already arrived in Poland, with repatriates, among others. very precisely with members of the Polish Repatriates Association, when during their national reunion were filled with surveys, expectations, we also wanted to capture all the inconveniences of the legal solutions to date. This law was also consulted in the environment of people waiting for repatriation, i.e. in Kazakhstan, because there is the largest group of Poles waiting for repatriation. In addition, the government has decided to introduce a group of repatriates, let's call them a pilot group to show what problems are ahead of us. Such a group was brought in on 20 December 2016 and these people as if they were going in such a little experimental mode, a system, but according to the new law. As far as the provisions of the new law are concerned, what is different from the original law is, firstly, a change in the definition of the repatriate. This means that the repatriant will be a person of Polish origin, deported - that is, as so far - by the authorities of the Soviet Union, who lived before the entry into force of this law in the Asian parts of the former republics of the Soviet Union. It is a novelty here to extend the definition of a repatriate to a spouse who is not necessarily a person of Polish origin. The experience so far showed that even if the entire repatriate family came to Poland, the people of Polish origin were subject to this definition, they had the appropriate help, while the person who was not Polish was a spouse, was deprived of any help, i.e. health care, later retirement benefits or other assistance. Thus, very often it happened that decisions on repatriation were either made by breaking up families, which is absolutely unacceptable, or that people resigned because they could not return entirely as a family. So this is one of the most important changes that significantly extends the definition of a repatriate. Of course, we are talking about deportees here, and in fact, this law defines the descendants of these deportees more. As these people are already directly deported, this is more than 80 years old, so for well-known reasons it is more relevant It will be descendants, not people directly deported. Another novelty in this bill is the appointment of a government representative for repatriation. It is about giving rank and someone having a name almost responsibility for the process of implementing this repatriation. A representative of the Government is to be appointed as Secretary or Undersecretary of State in the Ministry of Internal Affairs and Administration. Besides, until now the Ministry of Internal Affairs and Administration has been repatriating. Also a new subject, a new body... We provide for the appointment of a Repatial Council, which will be such a consultative and advisory body of the proxy, but at the same time will allow for systematisation, such organisation of many people who are actually engaged in repatriating completely socially so far. They are devoted to repatriation, so I think that in this Council for Repatriation they will have excellent opportunities to exploit their experience and commitment, which has so far been a little uncoordinated. Another novelty in the provisions of this law will be the appointment of adaptation centres, so at this point, if we are talking about the fact that we want to bring these repatriates, we want to help them, we want to do it in an organized manner. If a group of repatriates were to come to the country, then they would have been in an adaptation facility for 3 and 6 months. These 3 months are necessary for, first of all, the completion of all documentation formalities, including. It's about offices, civil status records, citizenship confirmations. As in the case of the previous law, the fact of crossing the border gives citizenship to Polish repatriates, but formally, it is necessary to confirm this citizenship, it is about own documents, identity card, PESEL etc. All these are essential things for repatriates who want to start working, live normally in Poland. Of course, the most important thing is the very intensive learning of Polish language, because unfortunately most people of Polish origin during all these years... with Polish language already have problems. There are the oldest people who still know the Polish language, those who had previously had some contact with the Polish language, but yet the vast majority is not Polish language, so here this intensive learning of Polish language is necessary to ensure that assimilation in the country is effective and correct. So this is the adaptation facility. Of course, after 3 months repatriates, i.e. Polish citizens with documentation, with basic knowledge of Polish language will be able to acquire apartments, jobs, etc. It is expected that stay in such a facility should not last more than 6 months. These 3 months represent a period for the departure from the adaptation centre, allowing for settlement and employment. Another may not be new, but some improvements in this law - we want to make procedural improvements with regard to consulates, clearly and precisely define the evidence confirming the conditions for repatriation, the evidence defining the civil status file, as well as the circulation of documents, so that consulates can carry this out efficiently. Plenipotentiary and forms of assistance provided for in this Act. As I have emphasized, so far there have been such forms of aid: development and start-up benefits and housing aid for municipalities which invited nameless repatriates families. Now we want this housing aid first of all, because it is the most important, unify and align in all aspects, and allocate it actually directly to the repatriates. We want to determine the housing aid envisaged at 25,000. PLN per repatriate, or family, let's say, four people is 100,000. zł. The repatriates would have the choice and even the possibility of using this housing aid in a variety of ways. If they have an apartment for rent, they will receive a payment in rent just up to the limit of this amount of 25 thousand. zł, except that the payment in rent will not be granted for more than 10 years, i.e. the entire period of financial assistance relating to the apartment should not last longer than 10 years. Repatriates can use this aid to buy their own apartment or this aid will cover part of the cost of purchasing their own apartment. This aid will also benefit municipalities in the same amount. If the municipality provides housing to the repatriates, it will also be able to obtain the same financial assistance, i.e. 25 thousand. zł na repatriate. A similar character, but it's no longer like a new thing, it's development aid, and it's 6,000. zł per repatriate. And another one here, maybe more just... it's not a new thing, but it's updated and more conveniently written down, it's help - reimbursement of travel expenses. Since it's been a train ticket so far, here we estimate that it's... so far there's been a train ticket for transit and possibly a second value of a train ticket for carriage of property. At this point, we propose in this act that it be sort of a flat-rate - double-value railway ticket. If anyone wants to bring a lot of property and take a lot of packages with them, then a double train ticket. However, it is known that at the moment you are travelling by plane rather than by rail, so this double rail ticket will be more or less comparable to the price of the train ticket, so here is the possibility of using this aid flexibly for reimbursement of travel expenses. Another issue is to cover health insurance, i.e. health benefits. From the moment the repatriates cross the border, they will be provided with health benefits. Until there is a repatriate or registered at the employment office, or he does not take up a job, that means he will not pay a health contribution, it will be covered by the state budget, and then it will be obvious - as he will be subject to a health contribution, just as every citizen will be subject to healthcare. Another issue is, of course, a small difference, whereas the difference resulting from more of a definition, because the repatriates who arrive, if they get retirement age, will of course receive pensions. So far there has been a problem with repatriates, actually with people who are not Polish and were married to repatriates. They couldn't get retirement benefits, and that was also the problem that didn't allow for effective repatriation and caused family break-ups. So now, at the moment of repairing this definition of the repatriate and covering her spouses, people who are not Polish, at this moment they will also be covered by pension benefits. Unfortunately, with most countries, including Kazakhstan, where most Poles live, who want to return to the country, we do not have pension agreements, so we can only say that there will be information about the number of years of work that entitles them to retirement, while the funds behind these pensioners from Kazakhstan, so far, do not go. We hope that the improving diplomatic and political relations with Kazakhstan will also allow for the signing of such agreements and the transfer of these funds, but this act on repatriation is obviously not yet resolved, hence the size of pensions will probably not be large, but it will always be a guarantee, at least the minimum that all Polish citizens have. I think that these are, in general, new forms of assistance that will enable the process of repatriation to be carried out more efficiently. Of course, we assume that this process of repatriation, because it is already too late for at least 25 years, to carry out quite efficiently, so we assume that the funds for repatriation will be a little higher than they have been expected so far. Until now, the provision in the state budget for repatriation has been worth PLN 30 million and has been used to a very small extent in general. We assume that in the following years it will amount to PLN 50, 60 million, and if it is missing, it is always possible to increase the reserve according to needs during the year. There was never such a budgetary position that the reserve could not be raised, but we reserve such amounts, such a government of magnitude, which will allow financial development for the group of repatriates about 1500 a year. It is very difficult to really estimate what repatriate needs are, because there are different data, different needs and different estimates. It's said, for example. $30,000. Poles who would like to come and return to the country, but of course these are just statistical estimates. Unfortunately, experience from Kazakhstan, for example, has shown that even those who have applied for a return visa registered at the consulate for several years have not confirmed it at this time or there is no trace of it, perhaps a significant part of it has gone to Russia, other countries. Probably so, so these estimates are very different. I think we should be very careful to estimate that this could be between 3 and 10,000, and these are the numbers that we should be looking at really, but it's really hard at this point to say clearly how many people it's going to be. We assume that this process of repatriation will be very fast forward for three years, so that the consulates and all the services can carry it out calmly, but the law itself will, of course, continue, because it is difficult to say if someone decides in four years' time that they also have no right to return, but this acceleration should take place within three years. However, the bill is not determined that after many years it ends. So I hope that this bill will allow for the real acceleration of the process of repatriation, as the Prime Minister said, that it will not stop until the last Pole who wants to return to Poland has this opportunity. [[Applause]] I hope that this law will allow, and thus Poland as a state will repay its moral commitment to people who were exported to the Asian part of the former Soviet Union in 1936 and in the 1940s. We think that these people have waited long enough to return to Poland, the more so that, despite their long absence, despite changing generations, they still retain their Polish identity, for example. In the passports of the Kazakh Polish nationality, which was not so obvious and did not meet with praise and favor, they continued to cultivate traditions, customs and Polish language in many places, they still maintained the Catholic faith. And this process, and this is our moral commitment to Poles living in the East, who want to return to Poland, should be fulfilled. And this is our commitment, which we now want to complete and complete this process of repatriation. We owe it to all Poles waiting all these years to return to their beloved homeland. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-22-sejm-38-1.u327	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club of Law and Justice in relation to the government's bill on repatriation and some other laws, print No. 1378. The project is the response of the Polish government to the expectations of thousands of Polish citizens in the East, who were forced to be deported or displaced without the possibility of returning to their homeland as a result of the war and the communist regime. It aims to speed up the return of repatriates to the country and to introduce new forms of assistance, including financial assistance. The scope of these changes is the result of consultations with the repatriate community in Poland and abroad, as well as local authorities and organisations dealing with this issue. According to data, since 2007, the number of repatriates has been steadily decreasing, and the waiting period for resettlement in Poland can last up to 10 years. Between 2007 and 2012, the number of repatriates decreased by half. This was the case with complex procedures and rules that extended the procedure and, above all, the lack of the necessary conditions for settlement, such as housing or livelihood. According to the data of the Supreme Audit Chamber, repatriating at the current rate, taking into account the number of waiting people and the average number of displaced persons in a given year, would last more than 16 years. This is an image of the great negligence of the last 10 years. The report by NIK clearly indicated that in previous years actions in the form of resettlement actions of people declaring Polish origin did not lead to the creation of an effective system of legal, organisational and financial solutions in this area. Against all these problems comes the government's draft amendment to the law, which, in addition to improving legal mechanisms, offers specific financial tools to secure and facilitate the return of our fellow countrymen to their homeland, including their families and spouses who will receive the status of repatriate. The establishment of a government representative will serve to centralise and improve procedures. The rank of the Minister of Plenipotentiary will speed up the decision-making process and remove the dispersed responsibility in this area, which has slowed down proceedings. The Act also sets a very short, only 30-day deadline for the appointment of an advisory and advisory body, the Repatriation Council, which will support the government's representative. Thanks to the new solutions, the Polish state will assume responsibility for completing the process of repatriation, which so far has burdened local governments to a large extent, closing many people with the possibility of returning to the country due to quite strict requirements or lack of adequate housing facilities in the municipalities. The Act introduces the possibility of creating adaptation centres for repatriates, which seems to be extremely important in the face of huge housing problems related to coming to Poland. Thanks to the new person repatriated will have time to adapt and start a new life in Poland, in which the state will provide her with the opportunity to stay in a language school for nearly half a year, vocational training, integration classes or at last close access to health and education institutions. To those who show particular adaptation difficulties, the municipality will be able to allocate a supporter for a period of up to 2 years to help deal with current problems and ultimately to self-emancipate. The provisions on financial assistance remain key in the draft law. In addition to living in the resort, the repatriant will be able to receive 200 PLN per month, and will receive funding for the purchase or rental of the premises, up to a total of 25 thousand. PLN, and 6 thousand. PLN to cover the cost of renovation and adaptation of the dwelling. The lack of such support so far has often prevented the accommodation from being adapted to use, guaranteeing only the reimbursement of the costs itself. A lot of people couldn't afford that kind of spending. The project also guarantees tax exemptions from donations and inheritances for repatriated persons, who will be able to receive apartments given to them in succession or in a donation act by way of relief. It's a huge support to take possession of your own apartment. According to data from the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, about 10 thousand people are interested in repatriation to Poland. Therefore, the proposed short vacathio legis will allow the law to enter into force quickly from 1 May 2017, i.e. less than 1.5 months after the government passed the bill to the Sejm. Given the enormous social expectation of the amendment of the law [[♪ The bell ♪]] and the particular importance of the revised matter The Parliamentary Club of Law and Justice welcomes the draft and recommends that it be referred to further legislative work. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-22-sejm-38-1.u337	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I have the honour on behalf of the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party to present my position on the draft law amending the Law on repatriation and some other laws. The bill deals with certain solutions that will create the right conditions to return, settle in Poland to Polish people of origin. These are persons sent or deported by the Soviet Union authorities, among others. to Armenia, Azerbaijan, Georgia and Kazakhstan. I mean all these countries, the republics that have been mentioned, but above all, the Asian parts of the Russian Federation. The draft law proposes a number of regulatory provisions to unlock and accelerate the return of repatriates to Poland. Among other things, the definition of the repatriate and procedures related to the acceleration of the granting of the national visa for repatriation have been amended. Solutions are also envisaged to improve and facilitate the process of repatriation. A representative of the government for repatriation will be appointed and a Council for Repatriation will be set up. The Secretary or Under-Secretary of State of the Ministry of Internal Affairs and Administration will act as representative of the Government, which will increase the rank of the institution concerned. The most important tasks will be to coordinate the measures supporting the repatriates and to issue decisions on the granting of financial assistance to them. A representative will cooperate with the authorities of local government units and non-governmental organisations. The draft law provides for the establishment of adoption centres which may be run by social organisations, associations or other legal persons. In these centres, courses and adoption and integration activities will be provided, and stay in the centre will be limited: from 3 to 6 months. Staying in the centre at that time will be a new proof of fulfillment of conditions of settling in Poland. The project proposes a new form of financial assistance. The plenipotentiary will provide the rep after leaving the financial assistance centre, among others. 25,000. PLN for a surcharge to the apartment or rent to the renter of the apartment for a period not exceeding 10 years, aid of 6 000. zł for adaptation or renovation of the apartment. At the same time, a one-off grant from the state budget to 25 000 will be granted to the municipality that will provide the apartment. zł per person from the family. A new form of assistance will also be the possibility of being allocated by the municipality in which the repatriant lives, a supporting person. The new legislation also extends the catalogue of inheritance tax exemptions and donations acquired by the repatriator. The draft law proposes a number of new regulations to unlock the resettlement process, among others. amending the definition of the repatriate and amending the procedure for granting a national visa for repatriation. A maximum waiting time has been set for people who do not have so-called. conditions for settling - in principle no more than 3 years. The Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party is in favour of further work in committees. Thank you. Thanks to this, Polish citizenship will not only be able to obtain people who are repatriated, but also their spouses. This is undoubtedly a good solution to prevent the separation of families and to help those who yearn for their motherhood to make their return decisions. In accordance with the draft law, the President of the Council of Ministers will appoint a representative of the government for repatriation, whose task will be to coordinate all activities related to the return of our compatriots to their homeland. ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-22-sejm-38-1.u380	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! I know we have very little time, very much delay. I will try to speak briefly to explain the importance of this bill. The main objective of the proposed act on the restitution of national cultural objects is to transpose into Polish law Directive 2014/60 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 15 May 2014 on the return of cultural objects unlawfully removed from the territory of a Member State, amending Regulation No 1024/2012 of the European Union. Ladies and gentlemen, the draft law on the restitution of cultural goods in a comprehensive and uniform manner is intended primarily to regulate this area, this entire field of activity in the country, in particular by introducing key categories and concepts into Polish legislation, and by regulating the competence and general competence of bodies in restitution matters. It should be noted that the draft law was very complicated in nature, because the directive covers several different areas of law, including: civil property law, civil proceedings, provisions relating to the matter of specific laws, e.g. on court bailiffs, executions, on court costs, public international law, private international law, European Union law, substantive criminal law, criminal proceedings, customs law, administrative proceedings, antiquities protection law and specific laws: on museums, on national archival resources, on libraries, on the conduct and organisation of cultural activities. I could still list all these different rights, and because of the delay, I won't. The most important element of this law is that it shapes the concepts and institutions whose existence in national order assumed and established regulations of the European Union, first of all it introduces a key legal term - namely national cultural goods - which has not yet been introduced into Polish legislation. The Act also defines the competence and competence of bodies in matters of restitution of cultural goods. In this context, it is of particular importance that the draft rules on cooperation between the Minister responsible for Agriculture and Rural Development are laid down. culture and protection of national heritage with the Minister responsible for Foreign Affairs in cases where restitution takes place on the basis of standards of international law. The Act - I would also like to stress - goes beyond the matter of the protection of monuments, because the provisions of Directive 2014/60, which I have already mentioned, are not limited to restitution to monuments themselves, but relate primarily to various artifacts, which are precisely the term of a cultural good. Mr. Marshal, High Izbo, we know that the lack of regulation on the restitution of cultural goods undermines the effectiveness of this task, that it is indeed a very important task for the whole Polish country - especially for Poland, because, as we know, the restitution of cultural goods refers to these artifacts and monuments that were simply stolen during World War II. Surely we cannot fully undo the effects of the massive looting that took place during the years of the war, but above all we can mitigate them in some way and this is what the draft law is designed to do. I would just like to mention the activities of the Ministry of Culture and National Heritage, because we know that this was not just about the war itself and the activities of the invaders, but also about the fatal politics of the Polish People's Republic, because basically restitution measures ceased in the 1950s and it was possible to initiate these actions after 1990. Since 1992, the Ministry of Culture has continuously collected data on war losses of the art department from the territory of the Republic of Poland and is taking action to recover them. I wanted to mention here that the record of lost works includes 23 departments, including painting, which is probably the most famous, but also: sculpture, graphics, furniture, fabrics, porcelain, glass, goldsmithy, militaria, numismatic and archaeological collections. So far 63 objects have been entered into this register of artifacts or cultural goods lost. We're looking for almost 7,000. paintings by Polish artists, including Aleksandr Gierymski, Jan Matejki, Jack Malczewski or Stanisław Wyspiański. Among the works of foreign painting are such names as Rubens, Rembrandt and Dürer. Polish war losses are also recorded by the Ministry of Culture and National Heritage in international databases such as Art Loss Register and Stoneworks of Art. This wide international cooperation has led to the return of 341 valuable objects, worth more than PLN 8 million, due to the efforts of the Ministry of Culture in the last five years. And this is where we come to this last purpose of the bill. Let us remember that the plundered works of art and cultural goods during the war are simply very valuable objects. They should return to their rightful owner, i.e. to the Republic of Poland, and this is what this law is supposed to serve. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-22-sejm-38-1.u382	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The draft law was implemented, as the Minister said, in the provisions of the EU directive on the return of cultural objects unlawfully removed from the territory of a Member State of the European Union. The aim of the Directive is to protect the integrity of the cultural heritage of each of the European Union countries by facilitating their investigation into the return of cultural goods illegally removed from their territories. The protection of cultural and national heritage, including one of the basic measures of this protection, which is the restitution of cultural goods, has not yet been regulated in a comprehensive and uniform manner in Poland. For this purpose, they are used, among others. regulations under civil law concerning the protection of property which, however, are not adapted to the specificities of such proceedings. The Act defines the authorities competent for the restitution of cultural objects carried out by the Republic of Poland on its territory and abroad, their tasks and powers, and introduces new categories and concepts in Polish legislation, such as national cultural goods, cultural goods or public collections. The draft law sets out the rules of procedure for the return from the European Union of Polish national cultural objects removed from our country in violation of the law. The project also contains provisions on the procedure for the return of cultural goods unlawfully removed from the European Union country from Poland. According to the project, the Minister of Culture and National Heritage will be responsible for the restitution of cultural goods. It is intended to take action at home and abroad to recover the lost cultural object. There have also been cases where the Minister has an obligation of office to deal with the matter of return of property. The Minister will also cooperate with the relevant institutions of the EU countries and support mutual consultation on the return of cultural goods within the European Union. As a result, Poland will be able to benefit from the simplified procedure of restitution of Polish national cultural goods established by European Union law. Provisions have also been introduced to improve the procedure for ordering the return of cultural objects in the territory of the EU country and eliminating the existing gaps and legal uncertainties. Legal regulations are foreseen to secure the found cultural property. Criminal provisions have also been introduced relating primarily to behaviour that hinders proceedings for the return of the foreign national cultural object. In short, it is a project that offers an opportunity for the most effective and faster recovery of the unlawful lost cultural goods. This is particularly important in the light of the continuing problems with the recovery of cultural goods lost by Poland as a result of World War II. The Law and Justice Club supports and recommends that the Law on the Restitution of National Cultural Goods be passed to the committee for further work. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-22-sejm-38-1.u383	Thank you very much, Member. Ms Anna Wasilewska, the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club, will now speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-22-sejm-38-1.u384	Mr. Marshal! Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! I am pleased to present the position of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club regarding the government draft law on the restitution of national cultural goods, print No. 1371. The proposed law is intended to transpose into Polish law Directive 2014/60 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 15 May 2014 on the return of cultural objects unlawfully removed from the territory of a Member State. The obligation to adopt this law arises from the article. Article 19 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the Directive, which requires the Member States of the European Union to implement this Directive into national law. The aim is to protect the integrity of the cultural heritage of each Member State by facilitating countries' return of cultural objects illegally exported to another Member State. Currently, Polish law does not contain rules governing the restitution of cultural goods. Civil property protection regulations have been used for this purpose, but they are not adapted to the specifics of such proceedings. The bill introduces the legal definition of the cultural object and presents it as a monument, but also as a moving non-historical object, i.e. collections of contemporary art whose behaviour is in the public interest either because of artistic, historical or scientific value or because of its importance for heritage and cultural development. The second definition contained in the bill is public collections, i.e. collections, for which the period for initiating recovery proceedings was extended from 30 to 75 years. The bill also states that museums will be protected in the inventory of museums in the museum and library materials belonging to the national library resource and national cultural objects which are archival materials. Rules of procedure were laid down on the return from the European Union of Polish national cultural goods removed from our country in violation of the law, but there is also a chapter devoted to the return from Poland of cultural goods exported in violation of the law from countries other than the EU. According to the draft law, the Minister of Culture and National Heritage will be responsible for the restitution of cultural goods. It is the minister who is to take action in the country and abroad to recover cultural goods. The project specifies when the Minister is obliged to deal with the return of monuments permanently exported abroad. The Minister also has the right to cooperate with the institutions of the EU countries. The provisions of the Directive are mainly standards governing the powers of the Member States of the European Union seeking to protect their cultural heritage in the territory of the Republic of Poland. As a result, gaps in Polish law concerning the scope of the powers of the state authorities in restitution matters will be eliminated and Poland will be able to use the procedure of simplified restitution of Polish national cultural goods. The effectiveness of the protection of Polish cultural heritage will also be increased. In addition, the bill provides for the introduction for companies and persons involved in the marketing of cultural goods, the obligation to keep registration books and expert reports issued by them. There are doubts about the draft law, but I hope that it will be resolved during further legislative work. The Civic Platform Parliamentary Club will be in favour of further proceding the bill in committees. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-22-sejm-38-1.u387	Thank you very much. Now on behalf of the club, Mr Grzegorz Furgo. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-22-sejm-38-1.u388	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen, already during the interwar period, a cell was created at the Ministry of Foreign Affairs with the aim of restitution of cultural goods exported from Poland. Her work focused first on importing poloniums from Russia on the basis of the Russian Treaty of 1921. The Second World War interrupted this work, and at the same time caused the collection of cultural goods lost by institutional and private owners in Poland to grow to superb sizes. The war losses suffered by Poland in the field of cultural goods are extremely difficult to estimate, and it is often impossible. A significant part of the original source documentation, such as inventory books or inventories, has been exported or deliberately destroyed by the German occupation or Soviet troops, so full restoration of the pre-war resources of many leading collections and collections is unrealistic. The registration of damages suffered by the Polish culture began in September 1939 and was conducted throughout the occupation period. In 1944, Polish historians operating in the underground prepared the first aggregate lists covering the loss of both public and private collections. It was the first loss statement to take account of losses incurred by all owners, including Catholic churches and synagogues, as well as associations, museums and private collections. After the end of the war, this material became a basic collection for the loss register developed by the Bureau of Recovery and Compensations operating at the Ministry of Culture and Art, and was used by the Polish restitution mission looking for cultural goods exported from Poland and which could be located in the occupation zones in Germany and Austria. This office was wound up in 1951. Currently, the Ministry of Foreign Affairs estimates that the number of lost works of art, whether exported or destroyed, is about 516 thousand. objects, subject to the fact that this number cannot give up the real state due to the conscious action of the occupiers. It covers only those objects that were registered in the post-war years, focusing mainly on works of ancient art. Since 1992. The Ministry of Culture and Art registered 63 thousand. monuments lost from Poland within its current borders. The law we are discussing today is to fill the gap in Polish legislation on restitution of lost cultural goods. This huge number of lost objects, the deep sense of loss that we are experiencing together, confirms the need for regulations in force in each of Europe's countries. Until now, it has often been easier for the Polish state to block an ongoing auction with a target in the United States than in Europe. The Law on the Restitution of National Cultural Goods is primarily a transposition into Polish legislation of the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council of 15 May 2014 on the return of cultural goods unlawfully removed from the territory of a Member State of the European Community. The obligation to adopt a law arises from the article. Article 19 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 Directive, which requires the Member States of the European Union to implement the Directive into Polish law. The law is to adapt the existing Polish law to the new legal status in EU law. The general objective of the Directive and the Law on the Restitution of Cultural Goods is to regulate, harmonise and, above all, facilitate the recovery of cultural goods illegally exported to another country within the European Community. This law is intended to protect the integrity of the cultural heritage of each European State by establishing its priority in the exercise of the rights of its citizens and public institutions in the recovery of lost cultural goods. The Act establishes a legal mechanism for the recovery of a cultural object, which is carried out before a court of the State in whose territory the cultural good was carried out, at the request of the Member State seeking reimbursement. The other party to this proceeding is the current holder of the cultural object. An extremely important feature of this law is, from the point of view of Polish history, the establishment of the primacy of the protection of the cultural heritage of a Member State over the rights of the owner of the cultural object, because the purpose of EU regulation is to protect the cultural heritage of each individual Member State and the integrity of that heritage. On the other hand, it may also happen that a citizen who, as a result of crime lost [[♪ The bell ♪]] The good of culture that has been carried out, will bring...

ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-22-sejm-38-1.u420	Position of the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party vis-à-vis the government draft law on the restitution of national cultural goods, print No. 1371. Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, The draft law prepared by the Ministry of Culture is a necessity for the implementation of European Union legislation. It specifically concerns the directive on the return of cultural objects which have been carried out outside the territory of the country unlawfully. The rules are intended to protect the integrity of the cultural heritage of each EU country by facilitating their investigation into the return of cultural objects illegally removed from their territories. So far it is quite unclear, because there is no regulation regarding restitution of works of art, cultural goods. This is particularly important in the context of works lost as a result of World War II. Regulation by clarifying the definition of the cultural object in our assessment can encourage Poland in its efforts to recover the lost works. At the same time, the project places greater responsibility on the Ministry of Culture for actions relating to the recovery of cultural objects abroad - it is also about consultation with EU organisations and institutions. As a result, Poland will be able to benefit from the simplified procedure of restitution of Polish national cultural goods established by EU law. The new regulations will also protect museums (not being monuments) included in the inventory of the museum which is an institution of culture and library materials belonging to the national library resource. Export restrictions will therefore also be covered by e.g. Unprotected collections of contemporary art, which are often very valuable. The new rules will improve return procedures while introducing new obligations for the export of works of art abroad. In the assessment of the club PSL is a good direction. We will therefore support this bill.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-22-sejm-38-1.u421	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Polish Free and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the Government's draft law on the restitution of national cultural goods, print No. 1371. Poland, due to its geopolitical position and turbulent history over the centuries, has suffered many times in terms of destroying the living tissue of the nation, but also property damage and spoiling cultural goods. Our national achievements were regularly plundered and exported by invaders from the East and West. Many goods have been destroyed, many have also been lost irretrievably due to the destruction of documents and the impossibility of confirming their ownership. The state's policy to protect cultural heritage is extremely important in order to preserve and strengthen the identity of the nation. A disrespectful approach in this area can lead to pathology and threats we can see in Western Europe - in Germany or France. The terrorist threat and the lack of security in your own home are the result of multi-culti policy and the dilution of cultural identity. I believe that this phenomenon must be countered. The draft law submitted, which is a transposition of Directive 2014/60/EU of the European Parliament and of the Council, gives hope that the recovery of cultural goods constituting our historical heritage will become easier and will, as a result, contribute to strengthening national identity. During World War II, during the German occupation, our cultural goods, including moving monuments, were destroyed and robbed. The works of art belonging to the State and private individuals have been exported for years beyond the western border by German collectors. The entire loss was completed by the Russian wave of liberation of Polish lands. More efficient and faster recovery of lost cultural goods is not the sole objective of the law. The Act recognises both monuments and material objects that are not monuments but are important due to their artistic, historical, scientific or cultural value. Such a definition of cultural goods will allow protection against removal from Poland in the future, e.g. museums included in the inventory of the museum, collections of national library resources and unprotected collections of contemporary art. Closer documentation of the circulation of cultural goods and their property will certainly make it difficult to carry them out illegally and, in the event of a breach of law, quickly obtain information on the cultural objects subject to the offence. The bill states that the Minister of Culture and National Heritage will be responsible for the restitution of cultural goods. It is intended to take action - both at home and abroad - to recover the lost cultural object. There have also been cases where the Minister has an obligation of office to deal with the matter of return of property. The Minister will also cooperate with the relevant institutions of the EU countries and support mutual consultation on the return of cultural goods within the European Union. As a result, Poland will be able to benefit from the simplified procedure of restitution of Polish national cultural goods established by EU law. At this point, reference should be made to the legislative action of the European Parliament, which has recognised the problem of dispersing national cultural goods. The provisions of Directive 2014/60/EU, which underpin this draft law, will certainly increase the cultural integrity of the Member States of the European Union. The bill will increase the legal security of public, ecclesiastical, collections and private monuments in the event of theft or embezzlement. Action will be taken to prevent the recovery of lost goods by concealing or falsifying information about them. It is sad to note, however, that the question of regaining Polish cultural goods exported to the East or left by Poles in the East remains unresolved. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Members of the Free and Solidarnia Circle fully support the bill on the restitution of national cultural goods.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-22-sejm-38-1.u425	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Kukiz Club15, I have the honour to present a draft law amending the excise duty Act, which follows. The excise tax law proposes that the concept of passenger cars be deleted. This means that passenger cars will not be subject to this tax. Both imports into and exports from Poland of passenger cars would be exempt from excise duty. In the case of a passenger car, excise duties are subject to import, intra-Community acquisition, first sale in the territory of the country of a passenger car not previously registered in the territory of the country in accordance with traffic regulations. Currently, passenger cars with an engine capacity above 2 l are taxed at a rate of 18,6% on the basis of the tax base and those with a lower capacity are taxed at 3,1% calculated on the basis of the tax base. The tax base is the value of the car, the amount of the sale, purchase or customs value in import. The subject of taxation is a passenger car, understood as a motor vehicle, and other motor vehicles, but generally intended for passenger transport. Non-personal cars up to 3.5 t dmc, i.e. permissible total weight, shall not be subject to excise duty or transport tax. Question: "What is the definition of a passenger car according to tax regulations?" According to Art. 5a point 19a of the Act on PIT and art. 4a point 9a of the CIT Act on passenger cars is a vehicle within the meaning of road traffic regulations with a maximum permissible weight not exceeding 3,5 t, designed to carry no more than 9 persons including the driver. Economic growth, reindustrialisation stimulates demand for cars. Poles should be encouraged to buy vehicles that meet environmental standards. These opportunities need to be created rationally, without complications of life and the development of bureaucracy. The way to achieve the ecological outcome, i.e. to achieve climate objectives, is also the development of road infrastructure, including the replacement of the machine park of Poles. The average age of a car driving on Polish roads, according to the institutions that deal with it, car institutes, is 14 years old. The share of passenger cars in motor vehicles park in Poland is 75.5%. The number of these cars is estimated at almost 21 million registered units. Almost 94% passenger cars belong to individuals, citizens of our country. How will the elimination of excise duties stimulate the sale of new passenger cars and newer used cars? What benefits will you have? The benefits will be to increase the safety of road users. It is estimated that we will reduce the cost of road accidents, which currently amounts to PLN 30 billion per year. According to World Bank data, the National Health Fund spends 3 billion a year on the treatment of victims of accidents. This is the basic argument - the health of our citizens. The introduction of younger vehicles on the market will also benefit from the calculation of motor insurances OC and AC. The level of contributions to policies is expected to decrease due to the safety of mobile vehicles. Motorists practically maintain the state budget, but not only. The resolution of the removal of excise duties on passenger cars will give an impetus to increasing turnover for recycling companies. Recovery of re-use materials is supported by the National Fund for Environmental Protection and Water Management. Next thing. It should also be remembered that it is necessary to ensure the efficient use of cars owned by Poles. The idea of sharing means of transport is a solution to this idea. This enables widespread digitisation, computerisation of our lives. Kukiz.15 is against the fiscalisation of Poland. The implementation of the concept of removing excise duties on passenger cars will help to free up restrictions on access to the market for new and second-hand cars. This is practically the end of the division of the car sales sector into dealers, commissions and individuals. We are also convinced that this will reduce the process of reporting, registration in the country of passenger vehicles as heavy goods vehicles. In some extreme cases, Ferrari can be a truck. The provisions of Parliament should also provide businesses with convenient conditions for the marketing of services and products without any unauthorised action. It should be stressed that creating favourable terms for the purchase and sale transaction will reduce the final price of cars purchased by the citizen. There is such a thing as bringing second-hand vehicles from outside Poland, withdrawn from traffic, seriously damaged, considered by insurance companies as vehicles which have suffered total damage. The elimination of excise duties on passenger cars has long-term benefits. After fasting, it will no longer pay to bring scrap metal to Poland. This solution will encourage buyers to buy new or newer vehicles, but not classified as waste. That's another benefit. With regard to the above-mentioned issue, it is the role of the Polish state that vehicles purchased outside our country for use, for example. in parts were not registered in the country as full-value cars. In accordance with the view of the Supreme Administrative Court, the judgment of 15 October 2014 established that the acquisition of waste status by a vehicle depends on whether it can move on the roads, i.e. be useful in accordance with the original purpose, and therefore, we are against the introduction of waste into Poland, which is currently partly due to the fact that they are burdened with excise duties and crashed cars are much more attractive to buyers and are later repaired in Poland. In conclusion, cars are to be allowed to move smoothly, ecologically, modernly or more modernly than those which move, which are treated as full-value, but the purchase of these vehicles should be exempt from excise duty. Poles, citizens, drivers already bear sufficient costs of operating cars, e.g. by the taxes included in the fuel - you can boldly check - as well as by buying out very expensive motor insurance. It is also worth adding that the tax burden currently leads to registration of cars in neighbouring countries, and that is what is happening. In the south of Poland it is very often used to register a car, e.g. in the neighbouring Czech Republic. A car registered in the Czech Republic e.g. by Poles living in war. Silesian is purchased in the Czech Republic, where the Pole will pay the tax, where the Pole will register it, where the Pole pays the company for the review, where the Pole insurances it. The whole amount is allocated abroad and the car is used on Polish roads. It's also worth mentioning that... Let us give Polish companies dealing in motor vehicles, parts, accessories and repairs. That's $250,000. - such is the crowd of people directly involved in bringing in, selling second-hand cars, that is what workshops come to, that is what mechanics come to do, that is what wholesalers, parts come to do. Whole families live and generally reduce excise duty will increase in this sector in plus for the Polish country, which will translate into a return on new taxes in connection with the employment of new workers. The elimination of excise duties will also stimulate the value of exports of freight sub-category passenger cars, which currently amounts to 18.1%. The increase in sales of passenger cars will positively increase the revenue for the state budget due to the increase in VAT revenue, VAT on goods and services. For a reminder, e.g. Importing cars from the USA to Europe also includes customs clearance. Customs do not pay for cars imported from the European Union. And now what is the biggest Polish port? Hamburg. Because there are cars coming to Poland, Polish traders pay customs and tax in Germany because there is 19 percent VAT there, not 23 percent as in Poland, to just... 'Cause that's what's causing the excise duty. The bill provides for the elimination of excise duties on passenger cars. It aims to reduce, as we read in the explanatory memorandum, the total tax burden imposed on citizens. Currently Art. 1 The Act on excise duty reads as follows: The Act sets out the taxation of excise duties, hereinafter referred to as excise duties, excise goods and passenger cars, the organisation of trade in excise goods and the marking of excise marks. The second paragraph: Excise duty is the revenue of the state budget. The project proposes a different wording of the whole article. This is what he says: The Act defines excise duty taxation, hereinafter referred to as excise duty, excise goods - i.e. there are no more cars - the organisation of trade in excise goods, as well as the marking of excise marks. It follows from the summary of the wording of the provision currently in force and proposed that the latter contains the wording of the paragraph. 1 after deleting words and passenger cars. Against this background, the question arises why from the article. 1 The rule that excise duty is the revenue of the state budget has disappeared. He's gone. Thus, the projectors feel that not only should excise duty on cars be passed on to the state budget, but no other excise duty at all. It must be stressed that this means a reduction in the revenue of the state budget by around 20%, tax revenue by around 20%. Is that what the designers wanted to exclude from the state budget at all? This is the basic error of the project. And now in the justification of the project we read that Poland is the only country using excise duty on cars. It's just not true. Taxes related to the widely understood registration and entry into service of a passenger car similar to our excise duty are found in 19 out of the 28 European Union countries. In several countries these taxes are simply called excise duties - Bulgaria, Greece, Cyprus, Poland - in others there are very similar solutions based on separate regulations. So you can't say because it's treated as an argument that only in Poland is excise duty, so it shouldn't be, because we're a freak. No, he's in 19 out of 28 countries. Removal of excise duties on cars will result in a reduction in budget revenue. Let this awareness begin to dominate Poland as well. On the nearest Saturday, 25 March 2017 on the feast of the Lord's Annunciation in the Cathedral basilica in Sosnowiec, the inauguration of the 25th anniversary of the pine diocese will take place. At Holy Mass. at 3 p.m. 10.30 we will thank God for the past years of our diocese. The pinewood diocese was established on March 25, 1992 by the Holy Father John Paul II bulla.Totus Tuus Poloniae populus. The capital of the newly created diocese was Sosnowiec, the cathedral of the pine church of St. The Assumption of the Blessed Virgin Mary. The pine diocese was included in the Częstochowa metropolis. The bishop of the pine diocese was appointed the former Provincial Inspector of the Salesian province of Wrocław. Dr. Adam Śmigielski, while the appointments for bishops of the auxiliary diocese were given to Fr. Tadeusz Pieronek and Fr. Bishop Piotr Skucha. The patrons of the pine diocese were St. Brother Albert Chmielowski and St. Rafał Kalinowski. The diocese of pinenovice included the following villages and decanates located in their territory: from the former diocese of Częstochowa - Będzin, Czeladź, Dąbrowa Górnicza, Łaza, Sączów, Sosnowiec and Siewierz, from the diocese of Kielce - Olkus, Pilica and Sułoszów, and from the diocese of Krakow - my home town of Jaworzno. At the time of the establishment of the pine diocese, 152 parishes were located on its territory, and the number of parishes increased to 162 by 2010. In terms of ecclesiastical history, the lands that now form the pine diocese have a very long and rich common history. The area of today's diocese from the time of the baptism of the Polish state until the beginning of the 19th century belonged to the diocese of Krakow. In the western part of today's diocese from the Middle Ages to the end of the 18th century, there was a Siewers' dukeship owned by the bishops of Cracow. There are also many other facts about the strong relationships of today's pine diocese with Cracow. The professors of the Krakow Academy served as parish priests in the parish of St. Andrzej the Apostle in Olkus, and for a very short time the priest of the Olkus parish was the later Saint - John of Kęt. They were: youth ministry, Caritas diocesan and spiritual seminary. In carrying out the first of these tasks, the bishop created a separate youth paper in the diocesan curia, and also appointed the priests responsible for this pastoral ministry. On 11 June 1992, Fr. Bishop Adam Śmigielski founded the Caritas Diocese of Sosnowiec, based in Sosnowiec. Another institution whose functioning is extremely important for every diocese is a seminary. The decree on the emerging of the pine church seminary in Krakow was issued by Bishop Śmigielski on 4 June 1992. Among the various pastoral initiatives undertaken in the area of the Diocese of Sosnowiec, there is a special distinction among the Betlejem, or Christian Community House for the Homeless in my home town of Jaworzno, whose creator and animator is Fr. ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-22-sejm-38-1.u497	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The Polish government undertook to rebuild Poland's strength and sovereignty. This sovereignty, however, is difficult to talk about when the fourth power, which many today unreasonably speak of as being the first or superior power, remains in foreign hands. We're talking about the media, of course. Of the 10 important nationwide journals, two belong to German companies, including the largest, "Facts," Springer, and "Puls Biznesu" to the Swedish consortium. For comparison, in Germany none of the major titles has a foreign owner. The owners of the two most frequently listened radio stations in Poland are Germany and France, and the five largest news portals are Germany, Spain and the USA. In the case of regional press, almost all titles belong to one German company Verlagsgruppe Passau. Also almost all color magazines have owners from outside Poland. It is worth mentioning that after the German publishing company took over the regional press, its sales dropped, so it is difficult to say that the only motivation to buy newspapers was business motivation. Can you imagine that the programming lines of these editorial offices would be contrary to German policy? I don't think so. One example of the fact that titles appearing in Poland are relays of German politics is immigration. It's hard, for example. find in the press belonging to Passauer or Springer criticism of immigration policy pushed by Angela Merkel. Today we still have the right to accumulate in one hand a huge amount of newspapers, radio and television stations, as well as Internet portals, which means gathering enormous power in a few hands, in the hands of non-Polish, and in hands not subject to any social control. It is this power that values attitudes, gives judgments, promotes the way of life of cosmopolitan, easy and pleasant, uninterested in the fate of the country. Therefore, it is so important for the sovereignty of Poland that the demonopoliation and recolonization of the media is so important. Media reform in Poland began and so far ended with the establishment of national media. I believe that it is important to revise the rules that now allow for the concentration of many media in a single publication, in addition to foreign media. This is a denial of media pluralism, as well as a threat to the sovereignty of our homeland. We should take action towards the introduction of Polish capital into the media market in order to achieve a balance in such a sensitive sphere as the space of public information. Thank you. [[Applause]]
