ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-22-sejm-38-1.u499	Mr. Marshal! Gentlemen! I ask you to listen to this appeal: Science for Development. I have talked to my son Matthew many times about the importance of science in the Responsible Development Strategy. There will be no breakage of the medium development trap without innovation and modern technologies created by our scientists and Polish capital. Of course, it is necessary to ensure that science is properly financed, but this is not sufficient. It is equally important to base science on values such as truth, reliability and transparency. The pursuit of truth is the imperative of every scientist. Not career, and cognitive passion is the engine of discoveries and inventions. Transparency can be viewed as a certain dimension of truth and therefore deserves a special place in the hierarchy of values. Surely there are institutes and universities that maintain high moral and scientific standards. Unfortunately, however, there are also those where brave ideas and young, talented scientists have no chance of developing. Undisclosed and informal links from the times of the Polish People's Republic are still alive there. Therefore, I ask your scientists as the elite of our country to support the idea of "Science for Development," to support the demands necessary for the modernisation of the country. I appeal for: firstly, the absolute submission of science to the search for truth according to the sentiment of the American Plato, the sed magis amica veritas, and thus the promotion of attitudes aimed at maintaining high standards of research, the ravage of all private manifestations in science. Secondly, maintaining the highest ethical standards in scientific and teaching work. Eliminating people who have violated ethical principles in science or didactics. Thirdly, based scientific institutions on full transparency. The transparency of scientific workers has a fundamental impact on their independence and decisions. In particular, this concerns the disclosure of cooperation with communist services and any group, business and family links. Fourthly, supporting projects with the greatest scientific and practical potential. The optimal use of funding for science requires support for areas that offer the best chances of achieving scientific and implementation success. Fifthly, supporting the most talented young researchers. Removing barriers to employment and promoting talent by eliminating informal links arising from the previously identified risks. I have a deep hope that the scientific community will do everything to promote the idea of science for development through efforts to make the academic community transparent, honest and guided by the love of truth. Thank you so much for your attention. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-03-22-sejm-38-1.u502	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Today marks the 60th anniversary of Jack Kaczmarski's birth, who was born on 22 March 1957 in Warsaw. He attended a renowned Warsaw high school - XV High School of General Education. Narciszy Żmichowska. Then in 1980 he graduated from Polish Studies at the University of Warsaw, writing a paper from the literature of enlightenment. In parallel with his studies, Kaczmarski began his music career. The author of "Murów" was associated with Kabaret Pod Egida Jan Pietrzak. In 1979. Kaczmarski received the award at the Student Song Festival in Krakow for his song At the time of the introduction of martial law in Poland, on 13 December 1981, Kaczmarski stayed in France on a concert tour. He didn't decide to go back to the country. Later, he lived mainly from performances, and some of the profits were devoted to supporting the underground Solidarity. In 1984 he started working for Radio Free Europe in Munich. He worked there until 1994, when the Polish radio section was liquidated. In 1995, he settled in Australia, where he was diagnosed with advanced esophageal cancer in March 2002. Kaczmarski decided on expensive, complicated therapy in Austria, hoping to avoid surgery permanently damaging his vocal cords. Numerous concerts were organized with fundraisers. Unfortunately, he failed - despite promising initial effects after 2 years of fighting Kaczmarski's disease, he died in one of Gdańsk hospitals on 10 April 2010. Jacek Kaczmarski appeared in Polish post-war culture as the author of lyrics and song composer. Despite the great popularity of his work, this choice of species has not yet become an object of more extensive literary analysis. The situation has changed in recent years, as shown by numerous works devoted to the author. So it is hoped that Kaczmarski's place in literature will eventually be properly recognized.
